Laura Dudas faces possibility of prosecution over alleged campaign violations&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; James BagnallWed , 04 Mar 2020 22:50:12 **45;8211;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsInside the slow-rolling disaster of Ottawa 's $9-billion LRT projecthttp : **33;8258;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;8293;TOOLONG ... title= " Trains travel the Confederation Line of the Ottawa LRT system at Lees Station . " alt= " Trains travel the Confederation Line of the Ottawa LRT system at Lees Station . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;8329;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571411 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;8359;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the end , they set aside what complicated stuff this was , and went with their gut &amp;mdash ; because at some point , you have to trust your hired guns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But when the City of Ottawa finally launched the first of its approved two-phase , $9 billion-plus light rail system last Sept. 14 , those with deep knowledge of the decade-long project were understandably nervous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t just that large construction projects like this are inherently unpredictable . Everyone understood that trying to mesh together so many trades , technologies and timelines is more art than science . It&amp;rsquo ; s why contingency payments were built into the contracts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The problem was , this project also harboured additional layers of risk that cascaded in a manner rarely seen in the launch of a major transportation system . An investigation by this newspaper -- based on interviews with city officials , politicians , light rail experts and procurement specialists , along with an examination of thousands of pages of documentation filed with the city and other regulatory authorities over the past decade -- reveals no single issue was at fault in this problem-plagued rollout . Rather , this was a case of seemingly reasonable risks compounding in unpredictable ways thanks in part to compressed budgets and timetables . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;This is the story of how myriad parts of the project failed to come together as promised . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s easy to forget now the sheer novelty of the underlying premise of LRT . Ottawa was the first city in North America to swap out a bus transit system in favour of light rail &amp;mdash ; a demanding technical as well as logistical challenge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;8395;TOOLONG ... class= " aligncenter size-full wp-image-1571782 " src= " https : **34;8430;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 1519 " height= " 2439 " srcset= " https : **31;8466;TOOLONG ... 1519w , https : **39;8499;TOOLONG ... 69w , https : **39;8540;TOOLONG ... 187w , https : **39;8581;TOOLONG ... 768w " sizes= " ( max-width : 1519px ) 100vw , 1519px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adding to the risk , a light rail vehicle was selected that had never before seen service . Alstom , the French manufacturer , had re-engineered an older Citadis model to withstand North American winters . The new train would be tested first in Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Equally bold was the design of the LRT system itself , which when it&amp;rsquo ; s working moves more people , more quickly than any other light rail operation in North America . Moreover , the setup also depends on three passenger transfer stations &amp;mdash ; Tunney&amp;rsquo ; s Pasture , Hurdman and Blair &amp;mdash ; working almost perfectly until the second stage of the LRT project is able to ease the congestion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Most LRT systems are built on greenfield sites where ridership is not yet established and therefore traditionally built up gradually , &amp;rdquo ; says a light rail engineer and former advisor to the City of Ottawa on LRT technologies , who declined to be named out of concern his remarks would be misinterpreted . &amp;ldquo ; Here , the city is transforming one transportation mode in an established corridor to a higher capacity mode with a ridership which starts at one of the highest levels in North America . This is a real first . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8622;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa 's new LRT system was up and running for the public for the first time in Ottawa Saturday Sept 14 , 2019. " alt= " Ottawa 's new LRT system was up and running for the public for the first time in Ottawa Saturday Sept 14 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;8658;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571314 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But perhaps the biggest risk concerned personnel . Because the city has so little recent experience with light rail systems , it offloaded nearly all responsibility for the first phase of the project to Rideau Transit Group , a private sector consortium . RTG built key parts of its Ottawa workforce from scratch , including the part responsible for maintenance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Little wonder John Manconi , the city&amp;rsquo ; s transportation services boss , had warned just the week before LRT began service , &amp;ldquo ; You will never get a foolproof system . &amp;rdquo ; He explained the important thing was to have contingencies in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nearly six months after the launch , the city is exhausted . Commuters can cite by rote the many ways a rail car can be disabled &amp;mdash ; jammed doors , hobbled communications , unpredictable rail switches , disconnected power cables , disabled brakes and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The nearly 200 employees at Rideau Transit Group and its services arm , Rideau Transit Maintenance , &amp;nbsp ; are becoming physically fatigued thanks to the endless fixes that must be applied to the trains , the tracks and the rest of the system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class= " " &gt;Last Thursday , for instance , two trains were immobilized at Rideau and Tremblay stations , and subsequently removed from service . This left just seven trains running , or little more than half the number that should be in service . The same day , maintenance workers made repairs to the catenary systems that deliver power to the trains , even as the maintenance yard experienced power outages. &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oddly , it&amp;rsquo ; s not RTG that&amp;rsquo ; s feeling the wrath of public opinion . The chief executives of RTG&amp;rsquo ; s three equity partners &amp;mdash ; Geoff Smith of Toronto-based EllisDon , Marcelino Fern&amp;aacute ; ndez Verdes of Grupo ACS of Madrid and Ian Edwards of SNC-Lavalin , the Montreal engineering firm &amp;mdash ; rarely frequent Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Peter Lauch , the face of RTG in Ottawa , isn&amp;rsquo ; t much better known . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No , the people wearing this embarrassing launch are the politicians and city staff who , much to their frustration , have relatively little control over how the fixes are being applied . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jim Watson , the city&amp;rsquo ; s mayor since 2010 , badly wanted this to be a demonstration of what Ottawa can do right . LRT was to have been a model of private sector competence twinned with knowledgeable government oversight . This project was a decade in the making , managed by corporations that have built LRT systems all over the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , Watson and his fellow councillors have been left to mull just what went wrong and try to secure answers before the next even more expensive stages of this megaproject become infected as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8688;TOOLONG ... title= " Mayor of Ottawa Jim Watson sits down with Postmedia 's Jon Willing for an end-of decade interview Monday , Dec. 16 , 2019 at Ottawa City Hall . " alt= " Mayor of Ottawa Jim Watson sits down with Postmedia 's Jon Willing for an end-of decade interview Monday , Dec. 16 , 2019 at Ottawa City Hall . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;8724;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571288 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; What stands out in retrospect is the city&amp;rsquo ; s overwhelming dependence on the private sector for expertise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a posture borne of politics from another era. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the exception of the Trillium Line , a modest pilot project started in 2001 on existing CP Rail track , Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s last experience with commuter rail was 1959. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Calgary and Edmonton launched light rail systems in the late 1970s and early 1980s , Ottawa opted for a transit system that relied exclusively on buses . The Transitway began operations in 1983. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city&amp;rsquo ; s familiarity with rail technology and operations diminished substantially over the years . When it finally reconsidered the idea of LRT a decade ago , it&amp;nbsp ; established a rail implementation office with fewer than 30 employees . In a project of this magnitude , this meant it had to hire dozens of outside experts for help with everything from procurement to managing property for construction work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most crucially , the lack of skilled personnel led Ottawa to offload nearly all of the responsibility and risk for the multi-billion-dollar LRT project to a consortium with experience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The big puzzle is why RTG and its maintenance arm RTM haven&amp;rsquo ; t been up to the job so far . City councillors&amp;rsquo ; concern is building because the consortium is absolutely central to the performance of the LRT during this stage and next , in ways the public may not yet appreciate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8754;TOOLONG ... title= " A Century Park bound LRT train pulls into Central LRT station in Edmonton , on Tuesday , March 26 , 2019. " alt= " A Century Park bound LRT train pulls into Central LRT station in Edmonton , on Tuesday , March 26 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Ian Kucerak Ian Kucerak " **28;8790;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571305 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Consider first the financial incentives guiding RTG . Under the deal struck seven years ago between the city and Rideau Transit Group , Ottawa committed to 30 years&amp;rsquo ; worth of monthly payments for RTG &amp;mdash ; based on the consortium&amp;rsquo ; s ability to offer reliable service as defined in the contract . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those payments &amp;mdash ; nearly $5 million per month &amp;mdash ; are to cover maintenance services and provide cash to keep the system up to date . More importantly , as far as incentives go , the monthly stipend will allow RTG&amp;rsquo ; s three main partners &amp;mdash ; ACS Group , SNC-Lavalin and EllisDon &amp;mdash ; to recoup the $300 million in equity and long-term debt they invested in the project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But they won&amp;rsquo ; t get paid if the trains aren&amp;rsquo ; t running &amp;mdash ; and the cumulative losses so far have topped an estimated $20 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Failure to provide ( LRT ) service at the required levels results in reduced payments , not just for the maintenance service itself , but also on the outstanding financed amount , &amp;rdquo ; is how city staff framed the stakes for RTG in a report to city council late in 2012. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The upshot is that RTG and RTM had every incentive to build a rock solid system that is easy to maintain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , remarkably , this financial formula failed to produce the desired effect . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8820;TOOLONG ... title= " Ian Edwards , president of infrastructure , SNC Lavalin Group Inc. , speaks during the International Economic Forum Of The Americas in Montreal , Quebec , Canada , on Tuesday , June 13 , 2017. " alt= " Ian Edwards , president of infrastructure , SNC Lavalin Group Inc. , speaks during the International Economic Forum Of The Americas in Montreal , Quebec , Canada , on Tuesday , June 13 , 2017. " data-portal-copyright= " Christinne Muschi " **28;8856;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571296 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Procurement experts maintain this doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean the city was wrong to give&amp;nbsp ; so much responsibility to Rideau Transit Group through the 2013 contract to design , build , finance &lt;em&gt;and &lt;/em&gt; maintain the first stage of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s LRT. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Clearly it would be preferable not to have any performance issues , but under the procurement approach taken by the city , RTG is actually bearing nearly all the project risk , &amp;rdquo ; says James Paul , an infrastructure procurement management consultant and former chief executive of Defence Construction Canada . &amp;ldquo ; When you&amp;rsquo ; re dealing with large , complex projects incorporating lots of innovation , public-private partnerships like this are highly suited , &amp;rdquo ; says Paul , who notes he did not advise the city on the LRT project . &amp;ldquo ; Can you imagine the added concerns if the city was the one paying for all the fixes and delays ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city is not blameless in the LRT rollout debacle . It delayed too long before finally jump-starting the project , then pursued contradictory objectives of speed and low cost . &amp;nbsp ; This combination heightened the risks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city&amp;rsquo ; s justification for going with buses rather than rail in the early 1980s is at first glance puzzling . Planners considered the capital region&amp;rsquo ; s population , at 700,000 plus , too small for light rail &amp;mdash ; although it clearly was not a problem for the Alberta cities , which each supported a smaller population at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A bus transit system does have the advantage of flexibility , both in terms of routes and the number of vehicles that can be employed at different times of the day . The disadvantage compared to rail is that buses carry fewer passengers and move into and out of the downtown much less quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This was a problem as early as the 1990s , as long lines of OC Transpo buses choked the core at rush hour . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; City councillors did not at first see the urgency . The Trillium Line test bed was launched in 2001 , an eight-kilometre stretch linking Bayview and Greenboro stations . It was meant to provide insight into light rail operations but the single-track system offered few lessons . These trains were powered by diesel engines , not through an electrified system as is the case with the current LRT. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8886;TOOLONG ... title= " Blair Station was busy with LRT passengers coming from downtown Ottawa , September 16 , 2019. " alt= " Blair Station was busy with LRT passengers coming from downtown Ottawa , September 16 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Photo by Jean Levac/Postmedia News " **28;8922;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1466653 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; There followed nearly a decade of false starts and delays as councillors studied the possibilities . Mayor Bob Chiarelli championed an LRT route from Barrhaven to downtown . The city even awarded a consortium led by German conglomerate Siemens an $880 million contract to build it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But weeks after Larry O&amp;rsquo ; Brien scored an unexpected victory for mayor in 2006 , city council cancelled the Siemens deal and commissioned a task force to re-examine the best LRT routes for the capital region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was puzzled by Chiarelli&amp;rsquo ; s choice of the route , &amp;rdquo ; says O&amp;rsquo ; Brien , &amp;ldquo ; and I knew the system needed a downtown tunnel to really be effective . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The task force , led by former federal transportation minister David Collenette , laid out a long-term vision that would see commuter rail link most parts of the region , including Gatineau . A tunnel in the core was central to the scheme . Not only would it mean less congestion above ground and much greater capacity in the transit system , it would also permit quieter , more pleasant streetscapes in the heart of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s tourist district . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even so , it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t until late 2008 that council approved a revised transportation master plan that finally paved the way for LRT . The plan carried this warning : the Transitway would not be able to handle any more buses downtown after 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The consequence of years of delay in moving forward with the LRT project was now clearly in view . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When O&amp;rsquo ; Brien lost the mayor&amp;rsquo ; s race in 2010 to Jim Watson , the LRT project was well advanced though not yet a done deal . Council had signed off on initial plans for a 12.5-kilometre LRT route comprising 13 stations from Tunney&amp;rsquo ; s Pasture to Blair Road . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario and the federal government had each agreed to contribute up to $600 million , leaving the City of Ottawa to absorb $900 million in expenses . The total cost f &lt;span style= " color : #333333 ; " &gt;or &lt;span class= " " &gt;this first phase of&lt;/span&gt; the project was estimated at $2.1 billion , &lt;span class= " " &gt;excluding more than $1 billion ( net present value ) in maintenance payments over 30 years , and Watson wanted to keep it there . **26;8952;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Watson was determined to keep on budget . City staff realigned the LRT route to allow for a shallower , less expensive dig . They capped the length of station platforms at 150 metres rather than 180 metres . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It soon became clear the city was pushing objectives that are usually at odds &amp;mdash ; speed and low cost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Councillors on March 1 , 2011 directed city staff and their in-house advisors Capital Transit Partners &amp;ldquo ; to explore ways of accelerating LRT. &amp;rdquo ; The interim project schedule , put together the year before , had predicted LRT service in mid-2019. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Council was not only mindful of the downtown bus crunch predicted for 2017 , but it also wanted to show off some of the LRT&amp;rsquo ; s downtown stations for the 150th anniversary of Confederation on July 1 , 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the request for proposals issued in 2011 , the three technically qualified consortia for Stage 1 were urged to find ways of &amp;ldquo ; further accelerating&amp;rdquo ; the schedule . Eventually May 24 , 2018 became the initial target date for the launch of LRT. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a city with &lt;strong&gt; &lt;/strong&gt; no recent experience in building an LRT system , insisting on speed was a risk . The emphasis on keeping costs low simply added to it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The three finalists ranked virtually the same on technical merit . Rideau Transit Group won the overall contest with a significantly lower cost bid &amp;mdash ; it scored 892 overall out of 1,000 compared to 627 for the runner-up consortium led by Bouygues , Fiera , Colas and Brookfield. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not only that , the city had convinced the bidders to accept responsibility for risks associated with the construction phase with limited exceptions . The costs of dealing with sinkholes , which delayed progress on the LRT tunnel , may be one of these &amp;mdash ; the city and RTG disagree over who should absorb the extra expenses . Building in a dense urban area was also problematic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But construction risks weren&amp;rsquo ; t the only ones faced by Rideau Transit Group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;8980;TOOLONG ... title= " Trains travel the Confederation Line of the Ottawa LRT system at Lees Station . February 10 , 2020. " alt= " Trains travel the Confederation Line of the Ottawa LRT system at Lees Station . February 10 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;9016;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571313 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Belfast Yards facility in Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s east end was built from scratch on a 40-acre site to assemble rail vehicles and house a small army of nearly 200 technicians and administrators for the LRT project . About 150 work directly for Alstom , the train manufacturer . Most of these specialists maintain , test and re-engineer the trains , the tracks and related infrastructure under subcontracts. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On a frigid January day , John Manconi and Allan Hubley , a councillor and chair of the transit commission , paid a visit to Belfast Yards . This was not a social call . They were there to impress upon RTM chief executive Peter Lauch the urgency of getting Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s light rail system to operate with some consistency . RTM has to do a better job of cleaning the trains&amp;rsquo ; power insulators , Manconi could be heard telling Lauch. &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s too much dirt building up , &amp;rdquo ; Manconi said . &amp;ldquo ; You need to have better procedures in place . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lauch nodded in agreement , suggesting RTM had the matter in hand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two days later , Manconi and Hubley returned , their frustration palpable . &amp;ldquo ; The cleaning wasn&amp;rsquo ; t done , &amp;rdquo ; Hubley explained . &amp;ldquo ; They keep telling us things are done and they aren&amp;rsquo ; t , &amp;rdquo ; he added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This has been the biggest shock of the LRT rollout &amp;mdash ; that there are problems with something as basic as maintenance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the early days of this project , city staff and their hired advisers &amp;mdash ; Capital Transit Partners &amp;mdash ; had impressed upon councillors the exceptional pedigree of the winning bidder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rideau Transit Group&amp;rsquo ; s various partners and sub-contractors had been involved in dozens of public-private partnerships around the globe . These corporations had drilled 1,300 kilometres of tunnels and built 2,500 kilometres of track for rail and mass transit projects , most recently in Seville , Barcelona , Ankara and Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They knew this stuff . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nevertheless , while ACS , EllisDon and SNC-Lavalin may have done similar projects elsewhere , construction projects and maintenance operations are inherently local . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And their link with the Ottawa region in the early going was tenuous , at least at senior levels . A public summary of the design and construction phase of the LRT system , provided to city officials at the beginning of the project , lists two dozen &amp;ldquo ; key individuals&amp;rdquo ; assigned to manage the project on behalf of RTG . None was based in Ottawa , though they would wind up spending a lot of time here . SNC-Lavalin and ACS managers were headquartered in Vancouver and Toronto . The vehicle maintenance manager Nigel Freeman worked out of Chicago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of course , construction project managers are used to constant movement and mobilizing workforces on the fly . But the timing of LRT made assembling skilled teams more difficult . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Rideau Transit Group began laying the foundations for LRT in 2013 , it found itself competing for tradespeople who were busy at an unusually large number of other projects around Ottawa &amp;mdash ; from the $3-billion refurbishment of the Parliament Buildings to a makeover of the National Arts Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; RTG partners even competed against themselves for skills . The same consortium won a $5-billion deal in mid-2015 to build a light rail system across Eglinton Avenue in Toronto &amp;mdash ; a project that is running behind schedule . A shortage of skills drove up costs at both sites as the trades demanded more money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet , in the end , the stations got built , tracks and electrical systems were installed &amp;mdash ; late , to be sure , but the system last fall seemed in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That is , except for one important piece . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9046;TOOLONG ... title= " LRT near Bayview Station . " alt= " LRT near Bayview Station . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;9082;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571317 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of all the system&amp;rsquo ; s glitches to date , councillors least expected the ones involving the Alstom train itself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paris-based Alstom , which on Feb. 17 revealed a deal to buy Bombardier&amp;rsquo ; s train manufacturing operation for up to $8.9 billion , is a beast of a corporation . Even before that acquisition was announced , Alstom was a top supplier of trains &amp;mdash ; high-speed rail , mass transit , light rail &amp;mdash ; you name a niche , Alstom&amp;rsquo ; s got a model to supply it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Across all of its business lines , which include train signalling systems and maintenance services , Alstom last year employed 36,000 and generated sales of nearly $12 billion &amp;mdash ; about $5 billion of it railway cars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like its rivals Siemens , Hitachi and Bombardier , Alstom is constantly tinkering with the technologies that go into rail cars . The mantra now is green energy &amp;mdash ; brakes that return power to the system , lighter materials , electric power and better onboard communications . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So it was with the Citadis Spirit &amp;mdash ; the model it sold to Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two months before the city signed the LRT contract with Rideau Transit Group in 2013 , councillors were assured by staff that more than 1,500 Alstom Citadis vehicles had been sold worldwide and accumulated more than 245 million kilometres in service . Further , &amp;ldquo ; the Ottawa Citadis vehicle is designed for cold and heavy snow climate operations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Citadis platform is indeed very reliable . But the Spirit variant had been tweaked to accommodate deep winter , among other things . The risk associated with the redesign was scarcely acknowledged by city staff . It was also downplayed by Alstom. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guillaume Mehlman , the&amp;nbsp ; managing director of Alstom Transport in North America , explained early on that to create the Spirit , his company had adapted the Citadis Dualis vehicle , then in service in Nantes and Lyons . He said changes had been made to the propulsion system in order to accommodate different braking and acceleration &amp;ldquo ; parameters . &amp;rdquo ; Crucially , the vehicle&amp;rsquo ; s systems had been &amp;ldquo ; winterized&amp;rdquo ; to accommodate temperatures as low as &amp;ndash ; 40 C. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is not a huge redesign , &amp;rdquo ; he told the International Railway Journal in September 2013. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet it was an important one . The Spirit was to be the first Citadis model sold in North America &amp;mdash ; which made Ottawa the lead customer , with all of the uncertainties that that entailed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the cold weather features listed by city staff in a 2012 presentation to council : the Spirit vehicles &amp;ldquo ; have a proven capacity to operate in heavy snow , &amp;rdquo ; and the ability to operate despite the influence of salt , glycol and other products used to deal with snow and ice on roads and walkways. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Spirit&amp;rsquo ; s suitability for extreme weather proved exaggerated . In late January , John Manconi advised city councillors about the vehicles&amp;rsquo ; loss of power stemming from electrical issues on the train&amp;rsquo ; s roof . &amp;ldquo ; The vehicles appear to be more prone to these failures during wet or inclement weather , &amp;rdquo ; he noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whether the trains malfunctioned due more to design or maintenance flaws or some combination of the two isn&amp;rsquo ; t yet clear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9112;TOOLONG ... title= " John Manconi , OC Transpo , updates councillors at the FEDCO ( Finance and Economic Development Committee ) meeting on LRT. " alt= " John Manconi , OC Transpo , updates councillors at the FEDCO ( Finance and Economic Development Committee ) meeting on LRT. " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;9148;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571293 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Should RTG have proposed a more mature vehicle ? Not necessarily , says James Paul . &amp;ldquo ; Everybody wants the latest technology with innovation in control and environmental systems , and highly efficient operating costs . What if they had ordered a bunch of diesel cars like they have on the Trillium Line ? There likely would have been questions about why the city went with older technology in a less efficient vehicle . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One company with a good view on the matter is Metrolinx , which operates transit systems across the Greater Toronto Area . Metrolinx recently ordered 62 Alstom Citadis Spirit vehicles for use on LRT lines being built for Finch Avenue and the Hurontario Line linking Brampton and Mississauga. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Metrolinx is conducting design reviews with Alstom , &amp;rdquo ; says Anne Marie Aikins , senior media manager with Metrolinx. &amp;ldquo ; Any issues being identified in Ottawa are being addressed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aikins points out there&amp;rsquo ; s plenty of time for fixes because the new Alstom trains aren&amp;rsquo ; t due to begin service on Finch West until 2023 . Hurontario&amp;rsquo ; s start date is a year after that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This means Metrolinx and Alstom will be the beneficiaries of lessons learned ( in Ottawa ) , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aikins declined to say exactly what lessons are being learned , but a source familiar with Alstom&amp;rsquo ; s discussions at Metrolinx says Alstom is tweaking the Toronto prototype vehicles to deal with a series of technical issues that have plagued the Ottawa system &amp;mdash ; malfunctioning doors , brakes , power systems and communications between the vehicles and the LRT system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not unusual to see this sort of re-engineering , &amp;rdquo ; says the light rail consultant with experience on the Ottawa LRT project . &amp;ldquo ; Rail manufacturers don&amp;rsquo ; t have the luxury of a Ford production line of hundreds of thousands of vehicles . They build in small to medium batches , each one enhanced to reflect the experiences of running the previous model . There may be a kind of continuous improvement that would necessitate revisions , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Metrolinx will be dealing with Alstom one way or the other . Its Eglinton line will operate using Flexity trains from Bombardier which , barring a surprise ruling from Europe&amp;rsquo ; s regulators , will soon be part of Alstom. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is Alstom up to the job ? Certainly the French giant has plenty of resources around the globe , not to mention deep knowledge of LRT. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s needed , and fast , is a reboot of Rideau Transit Maintenance , which is very much a startup operation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Consider how this four-year-old company was put together . A sample of more than 70 LinkedIn profiles suggests the skill levels should be sufficient to keep Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s LRT system rolling . Mechanics , technicians and engineers are there in abundance . But many of these employees are young and short on direct experience . More than 40 per cent of the sample consists of recent graduates of Algonquin College , Carleton University or University of Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Relative inexperience isn&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily a problem if senior managers are up to the job or the workforce is seeded with enough experts . On this front , city managers have been pushing Alstom to bring experienced managers to Ottawa for longer stretches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Alstom has been pretty good about responding to requests to have technicians on site , &amp;rdquo ; city councillor Allan Hubley notes , &amp;ldquo ; but they&amp;rsquo ; re also very quick to move out when the particular issue is dealt with . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most knowledgeable Alstom engineers are in high demand at projects around the globe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9178;TOOLONG ... title= " The public riding the LRT system on launch day in September . " alt= " The public riding the LRT system on launch day in September . " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;9214;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571315 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a desperate measure , but necessary . When Rideau Transit Group revealed in January that it had hired British light rail consultant James Boyle , it confirmed the urgency of regaining control of the Ottawa LRT system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is no longer a question of salvaging the first of multiple phases . The&amp;nbsp ; reality is that the same players responsible for Stage 1 are also at the centre of things in LRT&amp;rsquo ; s $4.7-billion second phase &amp;mdash ; which will see 24 stations added to the system by 2025 , along with nearly 44 kilometres of additional track . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just how much RTG and its allies dominate can be seen in their share of total contracts doled out to date . The money flow comes in two separate streams . First , RTG , SNC-Lavalin and their subcontractors enjoy a nearly 60 per cent share of the $6.4 billion worth of construction work done or awarded . They also have a lock on 100 per cent of some $3 billion worth of maintenance services contracts . Overall , this collection of firms has been awarded nearly three-quarters of the value of work associated with Stages 1 and 2 . The influence of SNC-Lavalin , which owns 40 per cent of RTG &lt;em&gt;and&lt;/em&gt; won the combined $1.6 billion construction and maintenance services contract for the Trillium Line extension , is especially notable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( These figures are courtesy of Isabelle Jasmin , the deputy city treasurer . For clarity , she points out the construction contracts are valued in nominal dollars that include the impact of inflation , while the 30-year maintenance contracts are measured in net present value &amp;mdash ; basically , the sum of all future payments discounted by 3.5 per cent per year to account for inflation and other things , and taking into account upfront investments and residual value of the maintenance facilities . Jasmin adds that the net present value numbers include the capital payments that must be earned by RTG and SNC-Lavalin by providing reliable train service . These are $300 million and $136.3 million respectively for the Confederation Line and Trillium Line. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clearly , RTG , Rideau Transit Maintenance , SNC-Lavalin and Alstom must get their act together , and soon , or the larger project will be at risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Much of the city&amp;rsquo ; s deep dependence on RTG and its business allies&amp;nbsp ; is by design . It&amp;rsquo ; s the way the original project agreement for Stage 1 was written , according to a Feb. 6 , 2017 memo written by city&amp;rsquo ; s outside counsel , Norton Rose Fulbright . The lawyers advised that the city was within its rights to simply amend its Stage 1 contract with Rideau Transit Group to permit the purchase of the same Alstom trains and maintenance services for Stage 2 &amp;mdash ; at least , for the east-west extensions of the Confederation Line . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The rationale : it made little economic sense to have two separate contractors maintaining the same set of tracks , or to run two different sets of trains . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is why Alstom will be providing 72 of its Citadis Spirit vehicles by 2025 , and Rideau Transit Maintenance will be looking after the entire stretch of Confederation Line from Moodie Station to Trim . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;9244;TOOLONG ... title= " The LRT system platforms were full around 8:30 a.m. at Tunney 's Pasture Station in Ottawa . November 12 , 2019. " alt= " The LRT system platforms were full around 8:30 a.m. at Tunney 's Pasture Station in Ottawa . November 12 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;9280;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1571316 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The influence of SNC-Lavalin might have been even greater but for the fact the city excluded Rideau Transit Group from competing for the $2.57-billion Confederation Line east-west extension contract won by a consortium led by Kiewit Corp . of Nebraska and Vinci SA of Paris . Officials wanted a fair competition and this was a piece of the project that could be separated out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; SNC-Lavalin could have been eliminated from the Trillium Line extension project as well , but the city was advised by Norton Rose Fulbright that technical and other differences in the makeup of the Trillium Line meant the city could still conduct a fair competition to determine a supplier . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; The incumbent advantage for RTG as a potential bidder on the Trillium Line extension is far from clear , &amp;rdquo ; the law firm concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , the Trillium Line uses trains that rely on diesel engines for power , rather than drawing energy from overhead wires as is the case with the new Confederation Line . &amp;nbsp ; Accordingly , the line will rely on the Swiss-built Stadler train , which allows for a conversion from diesel to electrical propulsion . The Alstom Citadis Spirit vehicle doesn&amp;rsquo ; t operate under diesel power . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city has been roundly criticized for awarding the Stage 2 contracts seven months before LRT launched last fall . Not only was this long before it could be confident the first stage of the system worked , but councillors were angered about how SNC-Lavalin appeared to skate through the Stage 2 evaluations . The company was allowed to proceed to the financial evaluation despite failing to clear a minimum threshold in the technical ranking . Norton Rose Fulbright had advised that the city had the right to overrule its procurement officials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Should the city have waited another year before deciding the Stage 2 winners ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; James Paul , the former CEO of Defence Construction Canada , points out that was a difficult judgment call . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The city would have been saying last summer , &amp;lsquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t have perfection , but we have already experienced significant delays and the system has passed most of the testing , so we need to get this thing launched , ' &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; Real world experience gives you information that no amount of testing will give you and that you should be able to rapidly address during operation . Should you wait until all the bugs are gone ? No. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No one forecast the mess that is unfolding . Choices were made , some as long ago as 60 years , and now this is the system we have . Inside city hall , there is talk about ripping up the maintenance services contract with RTG and finding a replacement that can do better . But even if you supposed a qualified vendor could be found that is not already busy , the dislocations this would cause would make today&amp;rsquo ; s difficulties pale by comparison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The incentives are in place to force RTG to put the fixes in place &amp;mdash ; some $436 million in combined capital is at risk . But this is not just a case of trying to avoid even more financial injury . These global firms need to head off damage to their corporate reputations as well . If these incentives don&amp;rsquo ; t do the trick in the next few months , then we really are in trouble . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;9310;TOOLONG " **36;9341;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;9379;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;9414;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9450;TOOLONG ... health agency recommends vigilance and hygiene , not school closings , to control COVID-19&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9485;TOOLONG ... pretty good ' : MyGCPay application gets positive reviews from some public servants&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9520;TOOLONG ... Trillium Line to shut May 2 for two years of extension work , Carleton says&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; James BagnallWed , 04 Mar 2020 19:47:07 **45;9555;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsPoliticsLawless : Closing Mrs. Tiggy Winkle 's -- what kind of shopping choices are we making ? http : **33;9602;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;9637;TOOLONG ... title= " The Westboro location of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle 's posted a Facebook notice to announce that it would be closing . " alt= " The Westboro location of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle 's posted a Facebook notice to announce that it would be closing . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;9673;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1560939 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the news this week of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s , another iconic Ottawa chain , closing its doors , we as a community are again faced with the rapidly changing face of retail in this city . It is so easy these days to find ourselves forgetting much beloved small businesses until they announce their time is up . &amp;ldquo ; Oh , I loved that place ! &amp;rdquo ; we post to social media , even though , truthfully , we haven&amp;rsquo ; t been there since college . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We were adventurous and rebellious once , searching for the diamonds in the rough and balking at multi-national conglomerates . Now , we have become creatures of habit and convenience , too busy and distracted to stray from our routines . When companies such as Rideau Street Bakery or Mrs. Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s close their doors , we shake our heads at the &amp;ldquo ; end of an era&amp;rdquo ; and don&amp;rsquo ; t give it much more thought . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even when we don&amp;rsquo ; t explicitly forget small businesses , we are drawn to the lower prices that are so often found in big chains and online mega retailers . We forgo service , quality and supporting the local economy in favour of sheer quantity . We&amp;rsquo ; re out to get the most we can for our paycheques . And our dollars never seem to stretch far enough . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , we overlook the horror stories of these big retailers , their environmental impact , how terribly they treat their employees , the massive inequality and economic devastation they so often create wherever they roll up . We even ignore the impact this has on our own stagnating wages , all so we can own more things for less money . We idolize bargain hunters and big brands , refusing to give smaller chains a chance , all in the name of saving a few bucks . We know we should support small businesses , but we don&amp;rsquo ; t . And with online shopping we don&amp;rsquo ; t even have to see the impact of our purchases for ourselves ; we just click &amp;ldquo ; add to cart , &amp;rdquo ; and we can pursue status and our love of things guilt-free. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a time of disruption . Global instability and climate concerns are putting pressure on supply chains and production , whether that be through weather events , civil disruption or disease outbreaks such as COVID-19 . Social media shows us the human impact of the pursuit of cheap products , in the form of poor working conditions , forced labour or industrial accidents . We can no longer turn a blind eye to the very real impact of our market choices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;9703;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;9745;TOOLONG ... The worrisome remake of our traditional main **28;9780;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; And it is our choice . We can continue buying massive quantities of cheap product that we know won&amp;rsquo ; t last , that we know may have been made unethically with a huge hidden cost . We can purchase these from retailers and distributors who care for nothing more than the bottom line . And in so many cases these things we so covet will simply fill up our oversized houses and overflowing garages before ending up in a landfill or a shipping container destined for the Global South or the ocean . It&amp;rsquo ; s what so many of us do now . How many of our things live on only as an itemized line on our mounting credit card bill ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have to be like that . The path of our economy is not set in stone . We can choose to purchase less , but to purchase locally , sustainably or ethically . We can be those rebellious kids again , and we would all be better for it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is not to say that every large retailer is inherently evil and every small business deserves to be saved . I also do not discount the problems with poverty that our city faces , but the next time a beloved local institution closes its doors , we need to take a long look at our own shopping habits as we lament the loss . Because so often our own apathy and greed pulled the trigger , as much as any market force . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **28;9810;TOOLONG Lawless&lt;/strong&gt; is an analyst , sustainability advocate and freelance writer in Ottawa&lt;/em&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN OPINION&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9840;TOOLONG ... Looming legal aid crisis puts refugees at risk&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9875;TOOLONG ... Coronavirus , Corona beer and risk-averse consumers&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9910;TOOLONG ... and Rashid : Facial recognition software requires checks and balances&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Beth LawlessTue , 03 Mar 2020 16:42:58 **45;9945;TOOLONG ... BusinessEgan : The worrisome remake of our traditional main streetshttp : **33;9992;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;10027;TOOLONG ... title= " Eira MacDonell , general manager of Mrs Tiggy Winkle 's Toy Store on Bank Street , says the store will remain open until the end of March but will close for good after . " alt= " Eira MacDonell , general manager of Mrs Tiggy Winkle 's Toy Store on Bank Street , says the store will remain open until the end of March but will close for good after . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;10063;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1570872 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;10093;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Our traditional main streets still have lots of stores , squeezed in between all that coffee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there are some things harder and harder to find on Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s urban mains : bookstores , music shops , hardwares , department stores of any size , big grocery outlets , newsstands , movie theatres , shoe stores , old-style &amp;ldquo ; corner&amp;rdquo ; stores , and now toy stores . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The demise of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s is a blow to the entire city and a reminder that the main street of your youth is gone , never to come back . But cheer up , here&amp;rsquo ; s a $2 doughnut. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;10129;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10171;TOOLONG ... Tiggy Winkles to close all locations after four decades in the Ottawa toy **29;10206;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; A fitness centre beside a yoga studio , next to a chiropractor , adjacent to a Bridgehead , beside a bank below a condo , alongside a trendy restaurant that will die in two years , is not a far-off description of what you see along stretches of Wellington , Bank , Rideau or Elgin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sure , Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s was just an independent toy store &amp;mdash ; six outlets at its peak . But is its demise telling us something important about the neighbourhoods we&amp;rsquo ; re building and the perilous future of the family-owned independent , yet another canary in retail&amp;rsquo ; s coal mine ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eira MacDonell is the general manager of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s , which is closing the last of its stores &amp;mdash ; the flagship on Bank Street in the Glebe &amp;mdash ; this month . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She has been there for 38 of the store&amp;rsquo ; s 43 years . So it&amp;rsquo ; s been a life&amp;rsquo ; s work . She&amp;rsquo ; s not interested in blaming customers for changes in shopping habits but points to factors , some well-known , others not . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10237;TOOLONG ... title= " Eira MacDonell , general manager of Mrs Tiggy Winkles Toy Store on Bank Street , says the store will remain open until the end of March but will close for good soon thereafter . " alt= " Eira MacDonell , general manager of Mrs Tiggy Winkles Toy Store on Bank Street , says the store will remain open until the end of March but will close for good soon thereafter . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;10273;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1570875 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For one thing , everyone from Canadian Tire to Costco to Shoppers to Loblaws is selling toys . Platforms like Facebook and Kijiji have made the resale of toys that much easier . There are parent-led online sites where toys are swapped or given away for free . Nothing wrong with that , she says , but it all begins to add up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not to mention that today&amp;rsquo ; s child has a list for Santa that is heavy digital or electronic , which is not Tiggy Winkle&amp;rsquo ; s strength . And looming over everything is the spectre of online shopping which , for a small retailer , can be a killer on price , convenience and availability . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( Let&amp;rsquo ; s just call it &amp;ldquo ; ironic&amp;rdquo ; that all of those people sitting in coffee shops possibly ordering merchandise online are helping destroy the little store next door where &amp;mdash ; imagine the horror &amp;mdash ; they could just walk in , try it and take it home , delivery included ! ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacDonell was asked about whether the traditional main street suffers from the loss of family-owned independents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; 100 per cent agree . I know we are leaving big holes in both the Bank and Wellington neighbourhoods . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; The stores created &amp;ldquo ; an energy&amp;rdquo ; that you would translate to the street , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , customers were so sad at the news that a &amp;ldquo ; memory wall&amp;rdquo ; has been created in the store to showcase all the kind letters , notes and messages . &amp;ldquo ; Some of the letters from children just melt my heart . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Along a nearby stretch of Bank , there is another bit of news too timely not to mention . The city&amp;rsquo ; s longest-running independent music retailer , Compact Music , is closing one of its two locations to make way for a pot shop . And doesn&amp;rsquo ; t that say everything about the times we live in ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;10303;TOOLONG ... title= " Ian Boyd talks to a customer outside their Bank Street location . " alt= " Ian Boyd talks to a customer outside their Bank Street location . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;10339;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1567245 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some interesting statistics from a report done for the Wellington West BIA , which supports one of the more buzzworthy strips in the city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of the roughly 550 businesses , the leading category in 2018 was &amp;ldquo ; professional services&amp;rdquo ; at 30 per cent , followed by &amp;ldquo ; health and beauty&amp;rdquo ; at 18 per cent , restaurants at 15 and stores and boutiques at 13. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another way of putting that ? Increasingly , our traditional main streets are turning into service hubs , not places we buy goods . Maybe that&amp;rsquo ; s nothing to worry about . Or , if you&amp;rsquo ; re trying to buy a pair of shoes , a book or a box of nails , maybe it is. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dennis Van Staalduinen is executive director of the BIA . He worries about anything that hurts the bottom line &amp;mdash ; hits to revenue from societal trends like online shopping , or higher costs from rent or taxes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More people are an important part of the solution , he says . All those new residential towers &amp;mdash ; along or near LRT &amp;mdash ; represent hundreds of new customers and more foot traffic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; d be foolish to look at the trends towards urbanization and intensification and think ( main streets ) are going to go away anytime soon . But the face of the urban main street is going to change . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; You know , we could sit here all day and get nostalgic about businesses that are nevermore , from Joe Feller to Freiman&amp;rsquo ; s to Rankin&amp;rsquo ; s Hardware to the Rialto theatre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But this is not about nostalgia . It&amp;rsquo ; s about whether we&amp;rsquo ; re creating main streets where , aside from coffee and coriander , there&amp;rsquo ; s nothing much to buy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To contact Kelly Egan , please call 613-726-5896 or email &lt;a href= " **26;10369;TOOLONG " **33;10397;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **29;10432;TOOLONG " &gt; ... **26;10463;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10491;TOOLONG ... would a COVID-19 pandemic look like in Ottawa ? &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10526;TOOLONG ... obscured by competing statistics and spin in Ontario 's education wars&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10561;TOOLONG ... like the wild west ' : Almonte residents take aim at speeding sledders&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Kelly EganMon , 02 Mar 2020 22:42:48 **45;10596;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Thane Burnett " **28;10714;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1562323 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Via Rail says it expects to gradually resume passenger service early next week following weeks of interruptions by blockades. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The blockades were set up by protesters in support of members of the Wet&amp;rsquo ; suwet&amp;rsquo ; en First Nation in British Columbia fighting the progress of a natural gas pipeline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Via announced Friday afternoon that the popular Toronto-Montreal and Toronto-Ottawa routes were to resume partial service Tuesday , and the &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; national routes between Toronto and Vancouver will have two departures : one departing Toronto on Wednesday , the other leaving Vancouver on Friday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; is expected to remain out of service until at least n &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;ext Friday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The routes were closed in the face of blockades set up on tracks owned by CN Rail and used by Via . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The longest-standing barricade , set up by members of the Mohawk First Nation on its territory in Tyendinaga , near Belleville , Ont. , was taken down last Monday , when Ontario Provincial Police served a court injunction obtained by the rail operators . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ten protesters were arrested . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Smaller blockades across Canada , including one in Kahnawake , Que. , south of Montreal , which blocked commuter trains , have been stopped following injunctions. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of Friday , Via Rail said 940 trains had been cancelled because of the blockades , affecting more than 164,000 passengers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About 1,000 workers were laid off at the height of the protests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We continue to work with the infrastructure owner , CN Rail , on making sure that normal service can resume on all routes and that passengers can be welcomed back on board our trains as quickly as possible , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; Staff ReporterSat , 29 Feb 2020 21:44:16 **45;10849;TOOLONG ... NewsSenators continue to battle Melnyk 's ex-pilot over $13,000 , despite labour board rulinghttp : **34;10896;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;10932;TOOLONG ... title= " Senators owner Eugene Melnyk chats with defenceman Thomas Chabot , left , during the annual Eugene Melnyk Skate for Kids at Canadian Tire Centre on Dec. 20. " alt= " Senators owner Eugene Melnyk chats with defenceman Thomas Chabot , left , during the annual Eugene Melnyk Skate for Kids at Canadian Tire Centre on Dec. 20. " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;10968;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1538243 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Llewella Morgan submitted her resignation as chief pilot for Ottawa Senators owner Eugene Melnyk last May , she gave three weeks&amp;rsquo ; notice . Pretty standard stuff . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four days later , she picked up Melnyk at his home in Barbados and flew him to Ottawa . The following day &amp;mdash ; May 15 &amp;mdash ; she noticed that her pay since April 30 had not been deposited in her bank account . That was unusual . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After multiple inquiries , the pilot was told her services were no longer required and that she needn&amp;rsquo ; t show up at Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s private aircraft hanger for her next scheduled flight &amp;mdash ; a trip to ferry Melnyk to Toronto . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The circumstances surrounding Morgan&amp;rsquo ; s termination were the trigger for a legal skirmish that eventually wound up before Johanne Cav&amp;eacute ; , a vice-chair of the Ontario Labour Relations Board . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a 20-page ruling published this month , Cav&amp;eacute ; ordered Capital Sports &amp;amp ; Entertainment &amp;mdash ; the Senators&amp;rsquo ; operating firm &amp;mdash ; to pay Morgan $12,273.75 for unpaid wages , vacation pay and severance , in addition to a week&amp;rsquo ; s worth of unpaid benefits . At most , the cost to the team would be $13,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CS&amp;amp ; E hired Gowling WLG Canada lawyer Craig Stehr to guide the defence and the company&amp;rsquo ; s chief financial officer Brian Crombie provided testimony . The obvious question : Why would the company expend so much effort on what seems a relatively minor claim ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk , the Senators and Morgan did not respond to queries , but Melnyk&amp;rsquo ; s lawyer did . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We acknowledge the Board&amp;rsquo ; s recent ruling , &amp;rdquo ; Stehr said . &amp;ldquo ; However , as is their right under the rules of procedure and practice , the Ottawa Senators intend to appeal . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CS&amp;amp ; E had advanced a number of arguments as to why Morgan wasn&amp;rsquo ; t owed money ; Cav&amp;eacute ; dismissed the key ones . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the Board ruling , Crombie argued that Morgan was not entitled to her claim because she was not an employee of Capital Sports &amp;amp ; Entertainment . It did not set her hours or supervise her work , Crombie said , nor did CS&amp;amp ; E own a private plane . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in her decision , Cav&amp;eacute ; said Capital Sports was unequivocally Morgan&amp;rsquo ; s employer , adding that Melnyk was the one who directed her schedule and compensation arrangements . His use of the private jet for personal reasons unrelated to Senators&amp;rsquo ; hockey , for which he later reimbursed the company , had no bearing on the matter , Cav&amp;eacute ; said . And the fact the aircraft was leased through Flightpath &amp;ldquo ; does not determine the employment status of the pilot who operates the plane , &amp;rdquo ; the Board vice-chair concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the ruing , CS&amp;amp ; E agreed that Morgan hadn&amp;rsquo ; t been paid for work performed between May 1 and May 15. &amp;nbsp ; But the company argued it could withhold that amount to cover what it claimed was an outstanding balance related to Morgan&amp;rsquo ; s training . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In her ruling , Cav&amp;eacute ; said there was no balance owing &amp;mdash ; Morgan had satisfied her training obligations by staying with CS&amp;amp ; E for a minimum of six months . The pilot met that obligation 15 days before giving notice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Morgan testified that she tendered her resignation because the job required her to work nearly 19 days per month on average , compared with the 12 days per month she had expected to put in . Melnyk paid her a salary of $130,000 per year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The labour board has ordered Melnyk , through CS&amp;amp ; E , to pay Morgan $5,591.08 for time worked in May , and $4,182.67 for vacation pay . Because she was terminated prior to the end of her three-week notice , the board ruled she is also entitled to $2,500 in severance pay , or one week&amp;rsquo ; s salary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Morgan will not , however , be able to claim the cost of an air ticket from Ottawa to her home in Winnipeg , an expense she incurred the day she was terminated and CS&amp;amp ; E left her stranded . Cav&amp;eacute ; noted that a reimbursement such as this is an expense , not compensation , and therefore not covered by the Ontario Labour Relations Act . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10998;TOOLONG ... to expect if COVID-19 evolves from scattered outbreaks into a global pandemic&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11033;TOOLONG ... appears briefly at council , says he ' owes it to constituents ' to maintain his seat&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11068;TOOLONG ... Tiggy Winkles to close all locations after four decades in the Ottawa toy businesshttp : **32;11150;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11184;TOOLONG ... title= " The Westboro location of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle 's posted a Facebook notice to announce that it would be closing . " alt= " The Westboro location of Mrs. Tiggy Winkle 's posted a Facebook notice to announce that it would be closing . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;11220;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1560939 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;11250;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Beloved local toy shop Mrs. Tiggy Winkles announced plans Wednesday to close its last Ottawa store after more than 40 years in business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earlier this month , it told customers that its Westboro Village location would close on Feb. 29 . BusinessLocal NewsFeasey : Canada should learn global lessons on wireless regulationhttp : **30;11438;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;11470;TOOLONG ... title= " The CRTC 's challenge : How best to regulate competition in Canada 's wireless market ? " alt= " The CRTC 's challenge : How best to regulate competition in Canada 's wireless market ? " data-portal-copyright= " Chris Roussakis " **28;11506;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1561955 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; To get better , telecom regulators need to learn from their own mistakes and from their counterparts elsewhere in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet in its current inquiry into wireless markets in Canada , the &lt;a href= " https : **33;11536;TOOLONG ... Radio-television and Telecommunications Commission&lt;/a&gt; ( CRTC ) risks not only being out of step with the rest of the world but moving off in the opposite direction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CRTC is considering regulations that would oblige the three national wireless carriers , Bell , Rogers and TELUS , to provide services on their networks to &amp;ldquo ; mobile virtual network operators&amp;rdquo ; or MVNOs on terms which the CRTC itself would likely determine . The CRTC believes that , by doing so , the MVNOs will resell these services to Canadian consumers , and particularly low-income consumers , at lower prices than consumers currently pay . It hopes this would be a temporary measure until regional carriers build out their own networks and provide the network-based competition the CRTC ultimately seeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Regulators elsewhere in the world have more than 20 years of experience facing similar issues . In markets with a similar number of carriers , such as in Europe , Australia and New Zealand , there have been only a handful of instances where regulators have adopted the measures being considered by the CRTC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; European regulators effectively abandoned MVNOs as a remedy for competition concerns after 2007 ; the Australians rejected it three years ago ; and the New Zealanders late last year . Israel is sometimes cited as a country where MVNO regulation has driven lower prices but the most striking feature of the Israeli market is not the MVNOs but the number of network-based wireless carriers &amp;ndash ; currently five &amp;ndash ; in a country with a population of only nine million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today , only Norway provides an example of regulating for MVNOs in a way that the CRTC might claim as a precedent , but the regulated carrier has almost 60-per-cent market share in that case . European competition authorities considered MVNOs as a remedy for competition concerns in mergers between facilities-based carriers but they proved so difficult to implement and so ineffective that they have been abandoned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since regulation for MVNOs is so rare elsewhere in the world , we have limited evidence as to its effects . We have more evidence from wireline markets , which suggests that allowing competitors access to networks on regulated terms reduces the willingness to invest in them . There is no reason to suppose the same does not apply in wireless markets , where significant investments in new 5G technology are currently being contemplated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As to the prices consumers pay , the evidence now shows that the vast majority of the reductions in consumer prices in European wireless over the previous decade were the result of lower unit costs enabled by investments in new technologies rather than changes in the degree of retail competition such as might be associated with the entry of MVNOs . As a result , European regulators are today placing a greater emphasis on investment in new technologies in both wireless and wireline markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What evidence we have on MVNOs does not support the CRTC&amp;rsquo ; s assumptions . In Spain and Norway , where MVNO regulation was adopted , &amp;ldquo ; low cost&amp;rdquo ; MVNOs account for less than two per cent of the market . Other studies find no evidence that prices paid by consumers as a whole , or low-income consumers in particular , are lower as the number of MVNOs or their market share increases . The Netherlands has more than 70 MVNOs but also relatively high prices for low-income consumers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is , in summary , no evidence from the rest of the world to suggest that the measures currently being considered by the CRTC for MVNOs would lower prices or otherwise benefit wireless consumers , including those on low incomes . The CRTC would do better to learn the lessons of the last 20 years , follow international best practice and continue to prioritize competition between networks and investment in new wireless technologies such as 5G. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **31;11571;TOOLONG Fease&lt;/strong&gt;y has more than 25 years of experience in telecommunications regulation around the world , including as group director at Vodafone . He is currently a panel member at UK &lt;a href= " https : **33;11604;TOOLONG ... and Markets Authority&lt;/a&gt; and an expert adviser to Rogers Communications for the &lt;a href= " https : **33;11639;TOOLONG ... Wireless Review&lt;/a&gt;. &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;11674;TOOLONG ... We know how to do ' meaningful consultations ' with Indigenous Peoples . Canopy growth 's new CEO says , ' We need more focus and discipline ' &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Bruce DeachmanFri , 14 Feb 2020 22:18:33 **45;12170;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsBagnall : Canopy Growth 's new CEO -- ' We need more focus and discipline'http : **29;12217;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12248;TOOLONG ... title= " Cans are filled with drinks and sent down a line for labelling . " alt= " Cans are filled with drinks and sent down a line for labelling . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;12284;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1494949 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps the best way to consider the financial snapshot laid down on Valentine&amp;rsquo ; s Day by Canopy Growth is that it offers a glimpse of a bygone era. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Smiths Falls cannabis company won the lottery in the fall of 2018 when Constellation Brands , the U.S. beer , wine and spirits giant , invested $5.1 billion to acquire a strong minority of its shares . Under the tutelage of Bruce Linton &amp;mdash ; Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s founder and co-CEO until last July &amp;mdash ; the Smiths Falls firm went on a spending spree not seen in these parts since the telecom bubble of 2000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canopy Growth laid it all out in its detailed filing Friday . During the nine months ended Dec. 31 , Canopy Growth shelled out $549 million to fund company operations , $611 million to build out marijuana , bottling and other facilities , $511 million for the cash portion of acquisitions and $441 million for an option to acquire Acreage Holdings should the U.S. finally legalize marijuana . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the end of the year , Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s seemingly inexhaustible supply of cash had shrunk to $2.3 billion &amp;mdash ; sizeable , yes , but not if the spending continued at pace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s why Constellation Brands inserted itself into Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s affairs last summer . This coming year , it will make its influence felt even more , by tightening operations and making sure its investment will one day pay off . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A thorough strategic review is underway , &amp;rdquo ; said David Klein , the former chief financial officer of Constellation Brands who took over as Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s CEO last month . &amp;ldquo ; We will rightsize our business over the next 90 days &amp;hellip ; in a thoughtful and measured fashion . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Klein stressed that the idea was to focus strongly on niches and market segments where Canopy Growth can &amp;ldquo ; win , &amp;rdquo ; and not spread its effort across so many product lines . &amp;ldquo ; We need more focus and discipline , &amp;rdquo ; Klein added . &amp;ldquo ; We need to work across every line item . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That said , Klein &amp;mdash ; who has been joined on Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s board by two other senior Constellation Brands officials &amp;mdash ; said &amp;ldquo ; we need to move faster and make bolder moves in the U.S , &amp;rdquo ; suggesting he is prepared to invest at least some of Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s cash pile in bolstering the firm&amp;rsquo ; s retail presence south of the border . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What has Constellation Brands got so far for its massive investment ? &amp;nbsp ; A company that generated net revenues of $123.8 million in its third fiscal quarter ended Dec. 31 but also suffered a net loss of $124.2 million . Yes , sales were up substantially from $83.0 million during the same quarter a year earlier , but that period included just two-and-a-half months&amp;rsquo ; worth of recreational cannabis sales . Canada legalized recreational marijuana Oct. 17 , 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;12314;TOOLONG ... title= " Canopy Growth unveiled the company 's edible products at Hotel Arts in Calgary on Monday , December 9 , 2019. " alt= " Canopy Growth unveiled the company 's edible products at Hotel Arts in Calgary on Monday , December 9 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Darren Makowichuk " **28;12350;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1560664 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was that seminal event that prompted Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s once-in-a-lifetime investment surge . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drilling deeper into the company&amp;rsquo ; s numbers , as Klein certainly has , reveals a range of possibilities for generating new revenues , including cannabis-laced drinks , chocolates ( which are already selling ) and vaping products . Momentum is growing on the retail front &amp;mdash ; especially in Ontario , which until December had done the least to encourage the rollout of legal cannabis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canopy Growth reported that as of this week it was operating 28 retail stores under the Tweed and Tokyo Smoke banner and had acquired the licence or right to apply for a license in 45 more locations in Newfoundland , Manitoba , Saskatchewan and Alberta . The company said it has acquired nine multi-year licensing agreements for stores in Ontario and expects to begin operating them in May . The Ontario government has said it plans overall to allow new cannabis retailers at the rate of about 20 per month starting in April . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canopy Growth added it has submitted an application for a store at its headquarters in Smiths Falls . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the big question marks hanging over the company is where Klein will trim the workforce that Linton built up so aggressively . The company employed nearly 4,900 at the end of December , up substantially from 2,700 at yearend 2018 . More than 1,000 work at Smiths Falls and several hundred more are in Kanata. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , between Mar. 31 and Dec. 31 , Canopy Growth spent $207 million related to topping up employees&amp;rsquo ; pay with shares &amp;mdash ; up from $79 million during the same period a year earlier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not that many share options are worth a lot these days . Fewer than 25 per cent of the 33.6 million share options outstanding as of Dec. 31 were exercisable for less than $25.50 per share &amp;mdash ; roughly where the company&amp;rsquo ; s share price stood Thursday , before Klein revealed plans to rightsize the company . Canopy Growth&amp;rsquo ; s shares jumped 15 per cent in Friday morning trading . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Employees are thus left with the twin hope that Klein&amp;rsquo ; s strategies pay off , and that , if these do , they will still be around to reap their reward . &lt;/p&gt; James BagnallFri , 14 Feb 2020 17:57:19 **45;12380;TOOLONG ...
