BusinessLocal BusinessLocal NewsShopify shares finish up nearly eight per cent as firm tops Q4 forecastshttp : **31;12788;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;12821;TOOLONG ... title= " Shopify headquarters on Elgin Street in Ottawa . " alt= " Shopify headquarters on Elgin Street in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;12857;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1238769 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s share price was on steroids in early trading Wednesday following the release of better-than-expected fourth-quarter results . Shares were up roughly 20 per cent , reaching $593.89 on the New York Stock Exchange and C$786.07 on the TSX , before retreating to a still-healthy gain of nearly eight per cent on the day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was the 18th consecutive quarter in which Shopify has beat consensus estimates for revenues . But Amy Shapero , the company&amp;rsquo ; s chief financial officer , acknowledged the fourth quarter was something special . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; It was a spectacular quarter , &amp;rdquo ; she noted during a conference call Wednesday with financial analysts , &amp;ldquo ; Every part of the company was contributing . That&amp;rsquo ; s not something we expect to happen every quarter . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Revenues came in at $505.2 million ( all figures U.S. ) for the quarter ended Dec. 31 , up 47 per cent year over year , the company reported . The consensus forecast by independent analysts according to Reuters had called for a tally of $482 million . Shopify had led them to expect it would be in the range of $472 million to $482 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , reflecting Shapero&amp;rsquo ; s comment about the fourth quarter being unusually good , the company also revealed a relatively conservative revenue forecast for 2020 . Shopify expects these to be in the range of $2.13 billion to $2.16 billion . &amp;nbsp ; This is virtually the same as the pre-earnings consensus forecast by independent analysts of $2.1 billion . And it compares with total revenue of $1.578 billion for 2019. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; 2019 was a milestone year for us &lt;strong&gt; , &lt;/strong&gt; &amp;rdquo ; said CEO and co-founder Tobi L&amp;uuml ; tke , &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve earned the trust of more than one million merchants , &amp;rdquo ; he added in reference to the number of online shops now run using Shopify technology . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify makes its money by charging online merchants a monthly fee for its dashboard technology . It also earns revenue by helping merchants with various aspects of running an online business , including payments , shipping and financing and analytics. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Typical of companies chasing rapid growth , Shopify continues to post significant losses . &amp;nbsp ; The operating loss in the fourth quarter was $30.1 million . While that was better than the expected operating loss of $47 million to $57 million , it nevertheless represented a deterioration from the $9.5 million loss in the fourth quarter of 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not only that , Shopify on Wednesday signalled much bigger losses in the coming year as it continues with aggressive hiring &amp;mdash ; the company now employs more than 5,000 including about 1,000 at its Ottawa headquarters &amp;mdash ; and investments such as a new fulfillment network that will see it compete more directly against Amazon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify anticipates its operating loss for 2020 will be in the range of $324 million to $344 million , much of it the result of stock-based compensation costs . &amp;nbsp ; Shopify is fairly generous with its shares when it comes to paying employees . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify said Wednesday it will invest another $80 million in new offices , notably in Vancouver and Toronto , as well as in new applications , products and markets , especially overseas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last fall , the company announced it would invest $1 billion over five or so years in a new fulfilment network &amp;mdash ; staging areas that will allow Shopify to store and ship products on behalf of smaller businesses that want to offer their customers two-day delivery . It has been cautious on this front . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify said it would continue to move &amp;ldquo ; carefully&amp;rdquo ; to roll out the network and would add &amp;ldquo ; scale only at the rate at which service levels can be maintained . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shopify finished the year with $2.46 billion in its coffers , up from roughly $2 billion at year-end 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company on Wednesday offered investors a snapshot of a smoothly-run operation which appears to be handling the inevitable problems of rapid growth very well . &amp;nbsp ; The big question handing over investors is whether Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s share value has escaped its moorings. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At Wednesday&amp;rsquo ; s peak , the company&amp;rsquo ; s market value was nearly $69 billion on the NYSE and C$90 billion on the TSX. &amp;nbsp ; That&amp;rsquo ; s roughly 33 times the firm&amp;rsquo ; s projected sales for this year . Subtracting Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s cash mountain gives an enterprise value of $66.4 billion &amp;mdash ; 31.6 times revenues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; PI Financial analyst Gus Papageorgiou tackled the issue of Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s valuation on Feb. 10 , when the firm&amp;rsquo ; s share price was just $476.85 , implying an enterprise value of $51.3 billion &amp;mdash ; some 26 times his estimate for 2020 revenues . ( Shopify closed Wednesday at $531 per share. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These are very rich multiples which are vulnerable to collapse if even the slightest expectation is missed , &amp;rdquo ; he wrote , while advising clients to wait for a drop in the shares before acquiring some . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; We continue to believe Shopify has an unassailable competitive position in a market that is almost immeasurable , &amp;rdquo ; he added . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; However , at some point valuation matters . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not on Wednesday though . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;12887;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsTech-BizBroadbent and Jackson : National pharmacare would help Canadians cope with rapidly changing job benefitshttp : **32;13076;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13110;TOOLONG ... title= " Fewer and fewer Canadians have robust drug coverage . " alt= " Fewer and fewer Canadians have robust drug coverage . " data-portal-copyright= " MJPrototype " **28;13146;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1556108 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The influential life and health insurance industry contends that the great majority of Canadians have adequate prescription drug coverage . It is claimed that this is achieved through a combination of public insurance for the elderly and social assistance recipients , and private insurance for the working age population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to a &lt;a href= " https : **33;13176;TOOLONG ... parliamentary committee report , &lt;/a&gt; some 80 per cent of the workforce do have private insurance coverage . And fewer than 10 per cent of the population , mainly the working poor , have no drug coverage at all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This public/private mix is not , however , working well for Canadians . On top of the unnecessarily high and rising cost of private insurance , the same parliamentary report reveals that 88 per cent of private insurance plans have co-pays or annual deductibles , which means that drug costs are partly covered out-of-pocket . Further , as employer-sponsored drug plans are generally much better than plans paid for by individuals in terms of drugs covered and co-pays , the evidence also shows that private insurance coverage is well below average for younger and lower-income families . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Good employer plans certainly still exist , notably for full-time permanent employees working in the public sector and for large employers . Most unionized workers have decent coverage . But these kinds of jobs are increasingly difficult to find . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While hard data are lacking , it seems almost certain that employer plans are shrinking compared to more costly and less generous individual private insurance plans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Back in 2000 , Statistics Canada published &amp;ldquo ; Benefits of the Job , &amp;rdquo ; a detailed profile of employer health care plans . It found that just 50 per cent of all employees had comprehensive health , dental and life/disability insurance coverage , rising to 70 per cent for unionized workers , 58 per cent for full-time workers , and 57 per cent for those in permanent jobs . The large majority of Canadian workers , however , are not in unions . Just 30 per cent of non-union workers , 17 per cent of part-time workers and 14 per cent of temporary workers were covered by a comprehensive plan ( a small proportion had just one kind of coverage. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Statistics Canada no longer provide data on employer health plans . However , coverage has almost certainly eroded due to the changing job market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The so-called gig economy has increased from 5.5 per cent to 8.2 per cent of all workers over the last decade and will likely continue to increase . Looking at employees , between 2001 and 2018 , the unionization rate fell from 30.4 per cent to 28.7 per cent , and the proportion working full-time and in permanent jobs fell as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most striking is the sharp decline in the proportion of employees covered by a defined benefit workplace pension plan , which fell from 33.3 per cent to 25.2 per cent . This is a good indicator of the downward pressure on workplace benefits , which arises from rising costs , as is also the case with prescription drugs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It seems highly probable that the proportion of workers covered by employer drug plans has been falling , and that the quality of these plans is eroding . The labour movement strongly supports a national pharmacare program not just to provide coverage to those who lack it , but also to reduce cost pressures on employers that work against increasing wages at the bargaining table . When companies provide drug plans , they have less income to allocate for wages for their workers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Employers should also support a national pharmacare program that would reduce drug costs and shift them away from payroll costs . Total benefit costs , including pensions , can account for as much as one-third of total payroll costs for larger unionized employers . It is widely recognized that publicly funded medicare gives Canadian companies a significant cost advantage over American competitors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet business lobby groups seem to fall in behind the vested interests of the life and health insurance and pharmaceutical industries when it come to a national pharmacare program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The introduction of a universal , single-payer , pharmacare system has many good arguments to commend it . One of the most powerful , though least mentioned , is that the current employer-based benefit system is eroding . We should deal with that problem rather than pretending it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t exist . National pharmacare is needed as soon as possible before even more Canadians find themselves lacking the means to afford the drugs they and their families desperately need . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **26;13211;TOOLONG Broadbent&lt;/strong&gt; is the Chair of the Broadbent Institute , an Ottawa think-tank . &lt;strong&gt;Andrew Jackson&lt;/strong&gt; is the institute 's Senior Policy Adviser . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13239;TOOLONG ... skirts , disaster relief and highlighting medals as ' bling ' might bring more women into the military&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13274;TOOLONG ... paramedics to get new powers to treat palliative patients at home&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13309;TOOLONG ... angry after Ottawa care home locks door on aged mother , refuses to let her back from hospital&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Ed Broadbent , Andrew JacksonMon , 10 Feb 2020 12:22:15 **45;13344;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildRegion 's jobless rate slips in January due to reduced labour forcehttp : **35;13391;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13428;TOOLONG ... title= " Capital region unemployment rose to 4.4 per cent in November . " alt= " Capital region unemployment rose to 4.4 per cent in November . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;13464;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1502158 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The capital region&amp;rsquo ; s jobless rate dropped a bit to 4.3 per cent in January , compared with 4.4 per cent in December , but it wasn&amp;rsquo ; t due to a strong economy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The number of jobs actually decreased by a seasonally adjusted 11,600. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The jobless rate fell only because the labour force , which includes people looking for work , shrank an even faster 12,900. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By sector , the big job losses in the capital region in January were in health care ( down 4,700 ) , construction ( down 4,000 ) and retail ( off 2,600 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall , a net drop of 16,300 ( not seasonally adjusted ) for a total of 780,900. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Federal government employment rose by a net 2,700 to 163,500 &amp;mdash ; 21 per cent of the region&amp;rsquo ; s workforce . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13494;TOOLONG ... resale prices surge 19 per cent in January as tight supplies shrink even further&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13529;TOOLONG ... to receive $58 million for ' minimal ' changes to new Coast Guard ships&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;13564;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsOttawa resale prices surge 19 per cent in January as tight supplies shrink even furtherhttp : **33;13646;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;13681;TOOLONG ... title= " Properties have been selling quickly across Ottawa , not least because there are so few available . " alt= " Properties have been selling quickly across Ottawa , not least because there are so few available . " data-portal-copyright= " James Bagnall " **28;13717;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1344754 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The big story of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s real estate market for the past few years has been shrinking supply . Few people are putting their homes or condos up for sale until they have someplace to go . In January , the scarcity of listings plumbed a whole new depth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The result : the average price for residential resales soared 19.3 per cent to $516,200 while condo resale prices were up a nearly equally aggressive 19.1 per cent to $338,100 , according to data published Wednesday by the Ottawa Real Estate Board . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here&amp;rsquo ; s what&amp;rsquo ; s happened to supplies : at the end of January there were little more than 1,400 residential homes for sale , a sharp 35 per cent drop from a year earlier while the number of condos listed plummeted 63 per cent year over year to a shade more than 250 . And the totals in January 2019 , remember , were down substantially from the previous year . This , in a city with nearly 400,000 occupied dwellings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city&amp;rsquo ; s 3,000 real estate agents nevertheless didn&amp;rsquo ; t do too badly in January , because they didn&amp;rsquo ; t have to spend as long marketing the properties . Agents sold 558 residential units in January , down just eight per cent from a year earlier , and 222 condos , up nearly seven per cent from January 2019 . Overall , the number of resales in January was off five per cent from the same month a year ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;13747;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13782;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Residences sold in January had been listed an average of 55 days compared to 72 days for sales in January 2019 . Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s shares slipped about two per cent Wednesday on a day when the New York Stock Exchange composite index climbed 1.2 per cent and the tech-heavy Nasdaq exchange edged up 0.4 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The news that might have affected Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s market value Wednesday was the revelation of a leak to online publisher Betakit of a staff memo written by chief executive and founder Tobi L&amp;uuml ; tke. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The memo , sent to Shopify employees Tuesday , explained the company&amp;rsquo ; s chief marketing officer Jeff Weiser was no longer with the company , and that L&amp;uuml ; tke had reorganized his executive suite to more fully integrate marketing with Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s other units , including product development . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Craig Miller , the company&amp;rsquo ; s chief product officer , will now be responsible for marketing , a role he played in 2011 during Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s early days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;uuml ; tke said in his note to staff that Weiser&amp;rsquo ; s two-year-long stint at the firm had succeeded only in developing a marketing approach that &amp;ldquo ; didn&amp;rsquo ; t work , &amp;rdquo ; and that is was now time to &amp;ldquo ; reboot and take another route . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;14205;TOOLONG ... title= " Craig Miller joined Shopify in September 2011 and has been chief product officer since February 2017 . Shopify is expected to report next Wednesday a loss for the year of $1.43 per share ( all figures U.S. ) on revenues of $1.56 billion , according to the consensus estimate calculated by Thomson Reuters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company is expected in its regular quarterly conference call to shed light for the first time on its forecast for 2020. &amp;nbsp ; Analysts for the moment are predicting a net loss of $1.27 per share on revenues of $2.11 billion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since it first listed its shares nearly five years ago on the TSX and NYSE , Shopify has got by with relatively little conventional marketing . It earns a solid chunk of its revenues by selling subscriptions to a technology platform that allows entrepreneurs to quickly set up e-commerce websites. &amp;nbsp ; Shopify has since developed a number of other products and services ranging from shipping to pay technology . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Online entrepreneurs have tended to find Shopify , not the other way around . &amp;nbsp ; But now that the company is set to take on some seriously large competitors &amp;mdash ; think Amazon &amp;mdash ; L&amp;uuml ; tke has apparently determined it&amp;rsquo ; s time for an approach to marketing that will be truly distinctive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; L&amp;uuml ; tke has a history of moving relatively quickly to address what he sees as weaknesses &amp;mdash ; he did so early on when the company&amp;rsquo ; s mobile app was being disparaged by people trying to buy goods and services offered by Shopify&amp;rsquo ; s customers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The stakes are much higher now , too . &amp;nbsp ; A share price of $475 plus on the NYSE gives Shopify a market value of more than $55 billion &amp;mdash ; that&amp;rsquo ; s more than 26 times the company&amp;rsquo ; s projected revenues for this year . That&amp;rsquo ; s a very high multiple . &amp;nbsp ; It&amp;rsquo ; s basically saying that Shopify will not put a step wrong in the next few years , that it will continue to add international customers and large corporations at a fast clip , and roll out its fulfilment ( shipping ) centres flawlessly. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yes , this company could do it . It is currently delivering submarines for the Royal Canadian Navy as well as air ambulance and wildfire suppression services to the Province of Manitoba . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Babcock Canada was recognized for its focus on employee development , offering tuition subsidies up to $1,000 for current employees , mentoring opportunities , paid internships and co-op placements for students and recent grads &amp;mdash ; Babcock hires about 60 co-op students every year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;14527;TOOLONG ... title= " Keely Gregory , manager of internal communications in the offices of Babcock Canada , an engineering consultant company based in Ottawa . It is part of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Top 100 Employers project managed by Mediacorp Canada Inc. that recognizes employers with exceptional human resources programs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Adobe also provides employees generous maternity and parental leave . Above all else , it&amp;rsquo ; s important that they feel supported during big moments , &amp;rdquo ; Scott said . &amp;ldquo ; As a father of four &amp;mdash ; I can certainly relate to how precious those early days are . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meghan Reid , a psychologist with Canada Career Counselling , said companies who offer a generous maternity or parental leave often do well at retaining talent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is important , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; A lot of people in our society want to have children and need to have a source of income and a sense of purpose and having that leave is important to be able to balance those two aspects of life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said this can be particularly important for women in the workplace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It can hold them back , people have assumptions once you&amp;rsquo ; re a mother , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; When an employer effectively handles a leave and understands that this is just a part of life&amp;hellip ; I think that speaks well for the employer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Being recognized as a top employer in Ottawa was a great honour for the company , Scott said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;14807;TOOLONG ... title= " Mike Scott , Senior Director , Enterprise Support &amp; Ottawa Site Leader at Adobe . BusinessLocal NewsNational Capital Region 's Top EmployersBagnall : LRT 2 evaluations -- the other part of the storyhttp : **35;14920;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;14957;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa 's light rail transit system has been plagued by technical glitches and now , by questions about procurement fairness . " alt= " Ottawa 's light rail transit system has been plagued by technical glitches and now , by questions about procurement fairness . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;14993;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1546260 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lost amid the outrage over how SNC-Lavalin managed to secure a piece of the $4.6-billion project to expand Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s light rail transit system despite low technical marks is something more reassuring for taxpayers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lion&amp;rsquo ; s share of LRT Stage 2 &amp;mdash ; some $2.6 billion worth of work &amp;mdash ; is being handled by a consortium led by Kiewit Construction of Omaha , Neb. , and Vinci Construction of Paris . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the Kiewit group also failed to secure top grades in a technical evaluation , it cleared the minimum 70 per cent threshold by a considerable margin . This , combined with a competitive financial package , gave Kiewit and its partners the right to build the east-west component of Stage 2 , the project known as the Confederation Line extension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kiewit&amp;rsquo ; s team will add 27 kilometres of track and 16 new stations . SNC Lavalin is responsible for extending the north-south Trillium line under a $663 million fixed-price contract . It will build eight new stations . Add in nearly $1 billion for a long-term maintenance and services contract and you&amp;rsquo ; ve got $1.6 billion in total . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Examining how Kiewit won its contest makes SNC Lavalin&amp;rsquo ; s weak technical marks all the more egregious. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to documents released by the city late Thursday , Kiewit won after a very competitive battle against teams led by Spanish construction giant Ferrovial and U.S. behemoth Bechtel . The Ferrovial group scored 81.5 per cent for technical merit against 80.1 per cent for Kiewit and 77.4 per cent for Bechtel . Given the subjective quality of many of the items evaluated , this was a tight spread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Similarly , in the contest for the Trillium Line extension contract , a group led by Acciona of Spain scored just shy of 85 per cent on technical merit , while its closest rival , Colas Group of France , obtained 84 per cent . However , the bid by SNC-Lavalin garnered 63.6 per cent in the first go-round . Even after the city&amp;rsquo ; s evaluators were prompted by a bid evaluation committee to revise their assessment , SNC-Lavalin scored just 67.3 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was only because the city exercised its right to push SNC-Lavalin into the financial evaluation stage that the Montreal-based firm received a shot at winning it all . A low financial bid ensured that it did . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If there&amp;rsquo ; s any competitor that deserves sympathy for how things turned out , it&amp;rsquo ; s the French giant , Colas Group . &amp;nbsp ; It was also a key partner in the Ferrovial bid , which means it placed first and second in the two separate technical evaluations , but won nothing for its troubles . Of course , that may have been because it was reluctant to trim its prices . It&amp;rsquo ; s not clear how close the bids were overall because the city did not release the associated financial packages that composed half the total marks in these contests. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What is clear is that five of the six teams that bid on the two main Stage 2 contracts were judged to be competitive on technical merit , and roughly equally so . Then there was SNC-Lavalin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;15023;TOOLONG ... title= " The O-train 's Trillium Line stop at Carleton University . " alt= " The O-train 's Trillium Line stop at Carleton University . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;15059;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1546854 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of note is the membership of the separate evaluation teams . Three experts &amp;mdash ; Michael Morgan , Colleen Connelly and Jack D&amp;rsquo ; Andrea &amp;mdash ; were common to the Confederation Line and Trillium Line extension projects , so there was a good deal of continuity in the scoring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there were differences in the teams . The lead evaluator in the assessment that savaged SNC-Lavalin&amp;rsquo ; s capability was Peter Schwartzentruber , who 10 years ago founded Podium Projects Inc. , a consulting group specializing in delivering infrastructure projects . In 2005 and 2006 , he was a senior project manager for SNC-Lavalin on a project involving the Vancouver international airport . The city hired Schwartzentruber for his independence and expertise , and that was what it got . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the documents published this week by the city was one that revealed sharp differences between the technical evaluation team and the bid evaluation steering committee over how the bid should be scored . ( The steering committee did not know the identity of the bidder , but it might have been possible to deduce it . ) In the document summaries covering the four main technical aspects &amp;mdash ; general technical , design , construction and maintenance &amp;mdash ; SNC-Lavalin&amp;rsquo ; s weaknesses were extensively catalogued . Its strengths were barely mentioned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With the other two contenders , it was the other way around . In the Confederation Line evaluation , the pros and cons were more evenly spread about . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Given the huge stakes , it&amp;rsquo ; s difficult to believe that SNC-Lavalin , a very experienced bidder on public infrastructure projects , could have been so lacking in diligence . The evaluation committee said SNC-Lavalin hadn&amp;rsquo ; t bothered to sign up qualified people , offered a weak narrative or justification for its bid and was disturbingly vague in many of its responses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bid evaluation committee set up by the city pushed back on nearly all these items &amp;hellip ; and got its way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just why it felt the need to do so has never been explained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But at least the puzzle doesn&amp;rsquo ; t need to consume the entire Stage 2 rollout. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;15089;TOOLONG ... Staff there referred media inquiries to the Mennonite Central Committee , which operates Ten Thousand Villages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company announced the shutdown &amp;ldquo ; with a heavy heart&amp;rdquo ; on Tuesday afternoon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In this challenging retail environment , we recognize your commitment , support and dedication to the success of Ten Thousand Villages Canada , &amp;rdquo ; the company &lt;a href= " https : **33;15369;TOOLONG ... in a release&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;However , in spite of all our best efforts , we have been unable to achieve the level of sales that would continue to provide us with the ability to operate a sustainable business model . It is for this reason that Mennonite Central Committee Canada ( MCCC ) has made the difficult decision to close their social enterprise , Ten Thousand Villages Canada . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;The stores , along with the company 's distribution centre in New Hamburg , Ont. , its head office , and its online operation at Tenthousandvillages.ca will be closed by May 29 . No date has been set for when the Ottawa store will close . The store celebrated its 20th anniversary in October . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to its website , Ten Thousand Villages was founded in 1946 in Pennsylvania by an American Mennonite , Ruth Edna Byler , who bought and sold handiwork from women she had met while working to alleviate poverty in Puerto Rico . BusinessLocal NewsAre we in a housing bubble ? Not really . It just feels like it.http : **37;15556;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;15595;TOOLONG ... title= " Houses are selling quickly in Ottawa . " alt= " Houses are selling quickly in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " James Bagnall " **28;15631;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1344754 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the most enduring myths about the National Capital Region is that our economy is immune from the dramatic ebbs and flows experienced by the country&amp;rsquo ; s other major cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not . During the past quarter century we&amp;rsquo ; ve been hit by two dramatic downsizings of the federal government &amp;mdash ; one in the mid-1990s , the other in the early 2010s . Our economy has also been side-swiped by the telecom crash of 2001 and the collapse in 2009 of the largest private-sector employer , Nortel Networks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The region has been fortunate in one respect , however . The government and high-tech sectors &amp;mdash ; which together account for nearly 30 per cent of the total workforce &amp;mdash ; never shrank in tandem . When government employment fell in the 1990s , high-tech was resurgent . In the wake of Nortel&amp;rsquo ; s collapse and the global financial crisis , the Conservatives padded the government workforce to offset the damage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;15661;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;15696;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Which is why the capital region&amp;rsquo ; s economy gives the impression of calm , despite the frenzy below the surface . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But now something rather odd is happening . The key sectors are all growing at the same time . The result : a jobs boom which , in turn , has been a catalyst for the region&amp;rsquo ; s housing market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Consider the employment data released Friday by Statistics Canada . The federal agency estimated nearly 800,000 people were working in Ottawa-Gatineau in December , up 62,600 from a year earlier . That&amp;rsquo ; s a gain of 8.5 per cent , the highest among the country&amp;rsquo ; s 33 largest cities but for Brantford , where the job market grew nine per cent . In contrast , employment across the country expanded by just 1.9 per cent over the past year . In another sign of the capital region&amp;rsquo ; s strength , about 80 per cent of the jobs added were full-time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; High-tech accounted for about 4,000 net new jobs while the federal government contributed 16,000 &amp;mdash ; thus establishing Justin Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s bone fides as labour friendly . StatCan estimated the federal government employed 160,000 locally in December while the tech sector supported about 43,000 workers directly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Big shifts in Ottawa real estate in the past 3 yrs . Overall , ave . prices for residential resale properties were up 22% from 2016 to 2019 ( annual data ) , but the range of hikes was enormous -- 8 of the 10 biggest gainers were in the west . Half the bottom ten were in outlying areas . &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/KmcajJld76 " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- James Bagnall ( @JamesBagnall1 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;15732;TOOLONG ... 12 , **33;15767;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;15802;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;After 3 yrs of rapid price gains , here 's how average resale prices stood in Ottawa in 2019 . Seven districts now average more than $800,000 -- compared to just three districts in 2018 . Data courtesy of &lt;a href= " https : **27;15837;TOOLONG ? src=h ... &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/u0Gg8GkLJE " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- James Bagnall ( @JamesBagnall1 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;15866;TOOLONG ... 12 , **33;15901;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;15936;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the latest jobs data suggest these two economic engines are also stimulating hiring more widely . Over the past year the region&amp;rsquo ; s economy has added more than 16,000 professionals who supply technical expertise to federal departments and high-tech firms . This sector employed nearly 80,000 in December . Wholesalers and retailers combined for 14,000 net new hires ; hotels and restaurants added 8,400. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Little wonder that , despite a rapidly growing labour force ( which includes people looking for work ) the capital region&amp;rsquo ; s jobless rate was just 4.4 per cent in December , just above historic lows. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nor is the employment surge a one-year fluke . The capital region has added 74,100 jobs since December 2016 &amp;mdash ; up 10.2 per cent , tied for second with Toronto , behind only Barrie . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Plenty of people have been moving to Ottawa and Gatineau to take advantage of the hot job market . StatCan estimates the region&amp;rsquo ; s working age population grew by 25,000 last year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This , in turn , is fuelling new home construction and the resale market alike . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everybody is hiring &amp;mdash ; government , hospitals , high-tech , &amp;rdquo ; says Bill Meyer , who leads a team of Remax real estate agents . &amp;ldquo ; Students have bought up nearly all the condos , &amp;rdquo ; he adds . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a perfect storm for real estate&amp;rdquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Whether you measure it by the last year or last 3 years , employment growth in Ottawa has been fastest or nearly so among the country 's 33 largest cities . It 's what happens when high-tech and government are both in hiring mode . &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/JtvItd0jzz " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- James Bagnall ( @JamesBagnall1 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;15971;TOOLONG ... 12 , **33;16006;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;16041;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Real Estate Board last week reported the average price for residential resale properties was $500,000 &amp;mdash ; up 10.3 per cent year over year . Condos fetched an average $311,000 &amp;mdash ; a gain of 11.5 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At first glance these increases don&amp;rsquo ; t look sustainable , given that the region&amp;rsquo ; s economy is growing less than two per cent per year in real terms ( after deducting inflation ) and that developers have been adding 8,000 to 9,000 new houses , town homes and condos each year , which should help keep a lid on prices through the expanded supply . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , federal government employment has a disproportionate impact on the housing market , which experienced historically low price gains during the five years leading up to and following Jim Flaherty&amp;rsquo ; s deficit-busting budget in 2012 . There&amp;rsquo ; s some catch-up taking place now that government workers and contractors are more comfortable with the Liberals&amp;rsquo ; spending intentions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As for all those new houses and condos , the population is growing fast enough to absorb all the new construction , and then some . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Which has led to a squeeze on listings for existing homes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The last time we had this few homes for sale was January 2004 , &amp;rdquo ; says Meyer . Just 1,800 residential and condo units were being advertised in December , down 33 per cent from a year earlier . This , in a region with more than half a million homes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We used to tell people , sell first , then we&amp;rsquo ; ll buy , &amp;rdquo ; says Paul Rushforth , who owns the real estate firm that bears his name . &amp;ldquo ; But now you can&amp;rsquo ; t sell until you find something , and it&amp;rsquo ; s very difficult to find something . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The shortage of listings has produced what no home buyer wants &amp;mdash ; seemingly endless bidding wars . Meyer notes his group sold 217 properties last year , roughly two-thirds following a bidding war . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nevertheless , the market doesn&amp;rsquo ; t appear to have lost its moorings yet , the way real estate did in Toronto and Vancouver in 2015 and 2016. &amp;ldquo ; The market didn&amp;rsquo ; t go crazy last year , &amp;rdquo ; Rushforth says . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s still no appetite for overpriced listings . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Indeed , the Ottawa Real Estate Board calculated the average gain for the combined residential and condo market last year was 8.4 per cent . &amp;nbsp ; While that was the highest since 2003 , it&amp;rsquo ; s nowhere near previous surges experienced in Ottawa . In the late 1960s , 1973-74 and 1983-84 , residential and condo prices soared 20 per cent or more . &amp;nbsp ; In 2002 , they jumped 14 per cent . No other year since has registered anything close to that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In short , we&amp;rsquo ; re still some distance from an employment and real estate bubble . But the momentum is certainly building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16076;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsTop job for new Sens CEO : Filling those empty seatshttp : **35;16228;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;16265;TOOLONG ... title= " FILE : Jim Little . " alt= " FILE : Jim Little . " data-portal-copyright= " Bill Brooks " **28;16301;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1537911 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The job facing Jim Little , the incoming chief executive of the Ottawa Senators , is quite simple : He has to bring back the fans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Going into Saturday&amp;rsquo ; s match against the Montreal Canadiens , the Senators had played 20 home games this season with average attendance of fewer than 11,800 , dead last in the 31-team National Hockey League , despite the fact many of those seats were filled by giving away tickets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a small market team like the Senators , this matters a lot financially . Because nearly 6,800 seats are unfilled each game , Senators owner Eugene Melnyk is leaving an estimated $500,000 on the table for every home match in the form of foregone ticket revenue . That&amp;rsquo ; s $20 million to date . For a team that barely broke even on operations last year on total revenues of $165 million &amp;mdash ; the recent estimate from Forbes Magazine &amp;mdash ; that gap must be addressed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Little , 55 , won&amp;rsquo ; t find it easy . The slide in attendance began during the 2016-17 season and has accelerated since then . Former top gun Cyril Leeder and chief marketing officer Peter O&amp;rsquo ; Leary were casualties of that initial decline . Melnyk replaced Leeder , now CEO of Myers Automotive Group , with Tom Anselmi early in 2017 . O&amp;rsquo ; Leary was fired in late 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16331;TOOLONG ... title= " A file photo of Jim Little , named on Friday as new CEO of the Ottawa Senators . " alt= " A file photo of Jim Little , named on Friday as new CEO of the Ottawa Senators . " data-portal-copyright= " Bill Brooks " **28;16367;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1538240 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anselmi&amp;rsquo ; s mandate was to oversee negotiations related to the $600-million-plus project to build an NHL stadium on LeBreton Flats . But Anselmi resigned in early 2018 , when it became clear Melnyk and Trinity Development founder John Ruddy wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t come to terms on the overall $4-billion mega project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ever since , Melnyk has been both owner and acting CEO , watching with increasing frustration as many fans have simply stopped coming to Senators home games . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The reasons are many , ranging from a weak team to the lack of a downtown arena , &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;but a key one concerns Melnyk , a Barbados resident who has not endeared himself to the Ottawa community . More than two-thirds of Ottawa residents surveyed a year ago by Ekos Research Associates believed Menyk bore the brunt of responsibility for the collapse of the LeBreton project , while fewer than 40 per cent blamed Ruddy . Late in 2017 , Melnyk had mused about moving the team if fan support did n't improve . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before 2017 , the team regularly attracted more than 18,000 fans per game and maintained a solid core of 13,000 season-ticket holders . The latter are now substantially fewer in number . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the hunt for a new CEO , which began at the end of last season , Melnyk recognized he needed to hire someone who could rebuild links with the community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Leeder , who had been part of the group that brought the NHL back to Ottawa in 1992 , was known and generally liked throughout the capital region . He was on a first-name basis with nearly all the 125 companies and organizations that rented suites in the Canadian Tire Centre and knew very well the importance of maintaining good relations with the business community . The Senators depend on corporate clients to fill 35 per cent of the seats every game , all the more important given that federal government employees are not allowed to accept tickets as gifts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16397;TOOLONG ... title= " Cyril Leeder on the day he left the employ of the Ottawa Senators in 2017. " alt= " Cyril Leeder on the day he left the employ of the Ottawa Senators in 2017. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;16433;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1538242 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Leeder , of course , had many years to develop these personal ties . Little will have to move much more quickly , but Melnyk thinks he&amp;rsquo ; s up to the job . Little was one of four candidates Melnyk interviewed in October , when a road trip took the Senators to Las Vegas . &amp;ldquo ; Jim stood out immediately , &amp;rdquo ; Melnyk wrote in a reply to queries by this newspaper . &amp;ldquo ; His passion for winning is apparent as soon as you meet him . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; LIttle , a McGill University arts graduate , is familiar with the world of sports thanks to a series of corporate marketing gigs . During the 2000s , he oversaw RBC&amp;rsquo ; s sponsorships for the Olympics and golf . Starting in 2012 , he took on the role of chief marketing officer for Calgary-based Shaw Communications , a cable operator and major promoter of sporting events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Little exited Shaw in May 2018 in the midst of a bone-crunching restructuring . Shaw&amp;rsquo ; s earnings in 2018 were hit by $446 million in restructuring costs related to the company&amp;rsquo ; s decision to trim operations in the face of a weakening consumer video market . Thirty-three hundred employees &amp;mdash ; Little among them &amp;mdash ; representing approximately 25 per cent of Shaw&amp;rsquo ; s total workforce , accepted buyouts. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Little had been one of Shaw&amp;rsquo ; s top 10 executives , the company&amp;rsquo ; s financial hiccup was not exclusively a marketing issue . In any case , Shaw&amp;rsquo ; s strategy of tightening up operations was evidently successful . Earnings soared to $753 million in its latest fiscal year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;16463;TOOLONG ... title= " Senators owner Eugene Melnyk chats with defenceman Thomas Chabot , left , during the annual Eugene Melnyk Skate for Kids at Canadian Tire Centre on Dec. 20. " alt= " Senators owner Eugene Melnyk chats with defenceman Thomas Chabot , left , during the annual Eugene Melnyk Skate for Kids at Canadian Tire Centre on Dec. 20. " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;16499;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1538243 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk noted that Little&amp;rsquo ; s mandate with the Senators would be to oversee operations at Canadian Tire Centre , &amp;ldquo ; including events , &amp;rdquo ; and to try to boost the team&amp;rsquo ; s revenues &amp;ldquo ; through partnerships and marketing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mostly , though , he has to start filling those seats with paying fans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16529;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsAfter the implosion : BlackBerry 's second chancehttp : **34;17256;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;17292;TOOLONG ... title= " Charles Eagan is chief technology officer of BlackBerry which will be unveiling new products aimed at the automotive market , at the Consumer Electronic Show in Las Vegas on Jan. 7 , 2020. " alt= " Charles Eagan is chief technology officer of BlackBerry which will be unveiling new products aimed at the automotive market , at the Consumer Electronic Show in Las Vegas on Jan. 7 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " PROVIDED " **28;17328;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1534629 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s still painful to contemplate what might have been . Ten years ago BlackBerry was at flood tide , the world&amp;rsquo ; s biggest maker of smartphones with annual sales of $20 billion ( all figure U.S. ) &amp;nbsp ; The Waterloo firm seemed well on its way to taking over from Nortel Networks as the country&amp;rsquo ; s flagship technology company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then , seemingly out of the blue , Apple roared past , sucking nearly all the life from the firm then known as Research in Motion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Apple&amp;rsquo ; s ascent was nearly vertical . Revenue from iPhone sales topped $142 billion in its most recent fiscal year , ended Sept. 30 . Meanwhile , BlackBerry is expected this fiscal year ( ending Feb. 29 ) to generate revenues of little more than $1 billion across all its product lines . Hardly any of it will involve smartphones. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For all of Nortel&amp;rsquo ; s strategic failings , the telecom equipment maker never let an opportunity **25;17358;TOOLONG big slip from its grasp . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet , BlackBerry lives on . It earned billions of dollars in profits before its reckoning with Apple &amp;mdash ; giving it the wherewithal to pay for a brutal downsizing and radical restructuring . BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s head count now stands at roughly 4,000 compared to nearly 18,000 at the peak . About 500 are based in Kanata , the home of QNX , which BlackBerry acquired in 2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;17385;TOOLONG ... title= " A BlackBerry smartphone. " alt= " A BlackBerry smartphone. " data-portal-copyright= " GLENN CHAPMAN " **28;17421;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1202022 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; BlackBerry redirected its expertise in wireless technologies towards corporations , hospitals and government , offering ways of securely linking all the electronics within . Its engineers then targeted the emerging Internet of Things &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; which , through 5G wireless technology , will make it possible to transform electronics in the home and car into networks accessible through smartphone apps . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The result of all this effort will be on display Tuesday morning in Las Vegas , at the Consumer Electronics Show . BlackBerry will be showcasing a multi-layered software platform that allows automakers to develop driver profiles , scan vehicles&amp;rsquo ; software systems for potential maintenance issues , and head off threats from hackers . The new suite of software products will also give automakers the ability to keep software up to date over a car&amp;rsquo ; s lifetime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s the first set of products developed with artificial intelligence and machine learning technology , which BlackBerry picked up a year ago when it shelled out $1.4 billion in cash and shares for California-based Cylance. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s technology is already embedded in more than 150 million vehicles worldwide , making it a leader in this niche . BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s operating system software , built by QNX , guides digital instrument panels , handsfree and entertainment systems , and helps avoid crashes . Audi , BMW , Ford , GM , Honda , Volkswagen &amp;mdash ; you name it , BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s in there , acting as the brain of the machine . QNX software is also central to efforts aimed at developing driverless vehicles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;17451;TOOLONG ... title= " An infotainment console is photographed inside a Mercedes-Benz in the BlackBerry QNX Autonomous Vehicle Innovation Centre ( AVIC ) at the Blackberry QNX headquarters in Ottawa Thursday , March 22 , 2018. " alt= " An infotainment console is photographed inside a Mercedes-Benz in the BlackBerry QNX Autonomous Vehicle Innovation Centre ( AVIC ) at the Blackberry QNX headquarters in Ottawa Thursday , March 22 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;17487;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340250 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Now , with Cylance software , the automakers can customize both the electronics contained within vehicles as well as the overall management of the software contained in the fleets of cars they sell . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The automobile is becoming a supercomputer on wheels , &amp;rdquo ; says Charles Eagan , BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s chief technology officer since June 2018. &amp;ldquo ; Securing software in autos is a massive opportunity for us because security is not something you retrofit . You try to design it in from the beginning . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eagan knows a fair bit about the power of BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s underlying technology &amp;mdash ; and the importance of combining it with artificial intelligence . A University of Waterloo grad ( applied math and electrical engineering ) , Eagan was a senior R&amp;amp ; D executive with QNX from 2005 to 2007 , when it was owned by Harman International . Eagan later worked at a series of tech firms before joining BlackBerry in 2011 &amp;mdash ; the year after it paid $200 million to purchase QNX from Harman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eagan led the development effort to forklift the QNX operating system into the BlackBerry 10 handset &amp;mdash ; the company&amp;rsquo ; s Hail Mary attempt to catch and surpass the iPhone all in one go . He remains proud of what the firm accomplished . &amp;ldquo ; We created the most advanced , secure smartphone ever , &amp;rdquo ; he says , &amp;ldquo ; and we did it in record time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , as the world now knows , it didn&amp;rsquo ; t matter . Apple&amp;rsquo ; s momentum by then was overwhelming . Eagan left BlackBerry in 2015 only to return again in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;17517;TOOLONG ... title= " An older QNX concept vehicle . " alt= " An older QNX concept vehicle . " data-portal-copyright= " PROVIDED " **28;17553;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1535073 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eagan is not one to dwell on the past . Based in Kanata , he is in charge of the company&amp;rsquo ; s recently-created BlackBerry Labs &amp;mdash ; a unit dedicated to commercializing products that combine technologies from the company&amp;rsquo ; s various divisions . A particular emphasis for the Labs&amp;rsquo ; 120 engineers will be the Internet of Things , and how to embed Cylance&amp;rsquo ; s artificial intelligence in the company&amp;rsquo ; s other product lines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The pressure on Eagan is intense . Not only are his competitors many , he&amp;rsquo ; s operating in an industry in which technology becomes obsolete relatively quickly . BlackBerry devotes slightly more than 25 per cent of its revenues on R&amp;amp ; D &amp;mdash ; even more than wireless technology rivals Nokia and Ericsson , which spend about 20 per cent of revenues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That , and the unusual spending associated with integrating Cylance&amp;rsquo ; s technology , accounted for BlackBerry&amp;rsquo ; s $111 million net loss during the nine months ended Nov. 30 , on revenues of $758 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The consensus forecast according to Thomson Reuters for fiscal 2021 , which begins Mar. 1 , calls for BlackBerry to produce a profit of nearly $100 million ( 16 cents per share ) on revenues of $1.2 billion . That&amp;rsquo ; s lower than the firm&amp;rsquo ; s profits from two years ago , but would nevertheless mark the beginning of a revival that received a catalyst this week in Las Vegas . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just don&amp;rsquo ; t dwell on where the company could be had it bought QNX sooner , and made more equal the early war against iPhone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;17583;TOOLONG ... " alt= " Less than one half of one per cent of Ottawa 's properties were listed for sale in December , making for a very tight market . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;17810;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1534738 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **33;17840;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s housing market appears trapped in a troublesome cycle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fewer than 1,800 residential homes and condominiums were listed for sale at the end of December , according to data published Monday by the Ottawa Real Estate Board . While this suggests reasonable options for buyers , the combined listings represent less than half of one per cent of the city&amp;rsquo ; s 400,000 residences . That&amp;rsquo ; s a tight market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Worse , as far as potential buyers are concerned , the number of residential for sale signs tumbled nearly 27 per cent year over year to 1,525 while condominiums available for resale were nearly impossible to find . Just 267 were listed in December , down a startling 57 per cent from a year earlier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;One more slice of Ottawa 's single family home resale market in 2019 . Compared to 2017 , residential resale listings are down 43 per cent while the number of available condos has slumped more than 70 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Here 's the breakdown by district for single family homes sold in Ottawa in 2019 -- average prices for houses resold compared to 2018 . Further analysis will appear in &lt;a href= " https : **33;18038;TOOLONG ... later today . &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/q4wJmf3lBJ " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- James Bagnall ( @JamesBagnall1 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;18073;TOOLONG ... 6 , **33;18108;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;18143;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; Somewhat surprisingly , Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s 3,000 realtors managed to sell nearly seven per cent more properties last year than they did in 2018 . The explanation : properties put up for sale simply sold much faster . Residential homes took 44 days on average in 2019 to find a buyer , compared to 57 days the previous year , while condos last year were snapped up in 45 days after initial listing , compared to 71 days in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The real estate board&amp;rsquo ; s outgoing president Dwight Delahunt said properties in certain popular districts &amp;ldquo ; were snapped up by prepared buyers&amp;rdquo ; almost immediately , often following bidding wars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Across the city there was wide variation in the residential resale market , ranging from a 31.5 per cent gain for the 36 properties sold last year in the district that includes Country Place , Pineglen and Crestview , to an eight per cent drop for the 109 residences sold in Bells Corners . Both of these extremes were influenced by unusual sales activity in a section of each district . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the Country Place district , six homes sold in the area north of West Hunt Club Road and west of the Rideau River for an average of $820,500 compared to two sales in this sub-district in 2018 , for just $644,000 . Even so , price gains throughout the wider district were robust . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Bells Corners district happens to include Cedar Hill and Orchard Estates , which in 2019 recorded just six sales averaging $1 million for each property compared to 12 sales in the $1 million-plus category during 2018 . Most price increases in other parts of the Bells Corners district ranged from nine per cent to 22 per cent year over year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall , 20 of the 46 real estate districts tracked by this newspaper registered average price rises in excess of 10 per cent with a heavy concentration in the western areas of the city . Among the geographical exceptions were Lower Town-Sandy Hill ( average price of $658,200 &amp;mdash ; up 12.2 per cent ) , Vanier ( $472,600 &amp;mdash ; up 11.3 per cent ) , Hunt Club-South Keys ( $470,000 &amp;mdash ; a gain of 11.9 per cent ) and Hunt Club-Windsor Park ( $526,400 , up 10.2 per cent ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of course , a few western districts also recorded year over year reversals or were among the smallest gainers . This included the district of Carlingwood-Westboro where sales of residential properties averaged $896,800 last year , down one per cent from the same period in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are now seven Ottawa districts in which average prices topped $800,000 , compared to just three districts in 2018. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;In advance of Monday 's December data release of house prices by &lt;a href= " https : **27;18178;TOOLONG ? src=h ... some national context provided by &lt;a href= " https : **29;18207;TOOLONG ? sr ...
