Mathieu Fleury : Let 's keep Le Droit to its Ontario rootshttp : **35;21613;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21650;TOOLONG ... title= " The Le Droit office in downtown Ottawa . " alt= " The Le Droit office in downtown Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;21686;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1524277 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; With an estimated 30,000 subscribers , most of them in Gatineau , Le Droit occupies an odd place in the world of Canadian journalism . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s largest French-language daily newspaper but also one of six regional papers in Groupe Capitales M&amp;eacute ; dias &amp;mdash ; the bankrupt firm founded in 2015 by Martin Cauchon , a former justice minister in the federal government of Jean Chr&amp;eacute ; tien . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The other five are Le Soleil of Qu&amp;eacute ; bec City , La Tribune of Sherbrooke , Le Quotidien of Saguenay , La Voix de l&amp;rsquo ; Est of Granby and Le Nouvelliste of Trois-Rivi&amp;egrave ; res . That , of course , makes Le Droit the only Ontario-based newspaper in the group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This might matter in the days ahead as the newspapers &amp;mdash ; now formed into a co-operative &amp;mdash ; prepare to emerge next week from the protection of the court following four months of reorganizing . Each newspaper has or will form an independent co-operative . However , all six will draw on common office services &amp;mdash ; pay , information technology , advertising &amp;mdash ; of a corporate entity that will serve as a kind of new parent company . This arrangement is expected to save costs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div id= " divtelariaoutstream " class= " slimcut " &gt; &lt;div id= " fsksplitbox252onscreen " class= " fsksplitbox252onscreen " &gt;Equally important , the Quebec-based newspapers have access to a $10-million-a-year fund announced last year by the Fran ? ois Legault government to support local journalism . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; In order to claim its part of this support , Le Droit has been mulling establishing its co-operative in Gatineau . Although Le Droit formed its co-operative Friday , the decision about where to locate it has yet to be made . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;21716;TOOLONG ... title= " Coun. Mathieu Fleury. " alt= " Coun. Mathieu Fleury. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;21752;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1412017 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patrick Duquette , president of Le Droit&amp;rsquo ; s union local , said last month that Le Droit would continue to maintain a presence in Ontario , noting that the newspaper was formed in 1913 to protect the rights of Franco-Ontarians. &amp;ldquo ; We would never deny our roots , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nevertheless , Duquette added Friday that moving a certain percentage of Le Droit&amp;rsquo ; s 65 employees to Gatineau might be necessary to qualify for Quebec tax credits and other forms of assistance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pierre Bergeron , the newspaper&amp;rsquo ; s publisher until 2002 , agrees Le Droit may have little choice but to shift employees from its current offices in the ByWard Market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; " The move to Gatineau , if it happens , will be because that 's where the money is , " Bergeron said . " Whatever happens , Le Droit has to survive so it can continue to serve readers on both sides of the river . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The possibility of shifting resources to Gatineau concerns Ottawa city Coun. Mathieu Fleury , who represents the strongly francophone ward of Rideau-Vanier. &amp;ldquo ; Le Droit is a very important newspaper for coverage of local affairs in French , especially provincial affairs , which we don&amp;rsquo ; t get elsewhere , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last week , Fleury tabled a notice of a motion asking council to acknowledge the importance of Le Droit for Ottawa and to encourage the newspaper to collaborate with the local community in a hunt for ways to keep Le Droit in Ontario . The motion is to be considered in January . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even before the financial difficulties of Le Droit&amp;rsquo ; s parent firm , GCM , became apparent last summer , Fleury had been concerned about the newspaper&amp;rsquo ; s disproportionate coverage of Gatineau compared with Ottawa . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s too heavy on Gatineau at the moment , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It has been that way for some time . Bergeron said that , when he stepped down as publisher in 2002 , about three-quarters of Le Droit&amp;rsquo ; s circulation &amp;mdash ; about 40,000 at the time &amp;mdash ; was on the Qu&amp;eacute ; bec side of the Ottawa River . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s possible the balance could have shifted somewhat in favour of Ottawa since then . According to the national census , the number of people with working knowledge of French increased nearly 57,000 between 2006 and 2016 in Ottawa , compared with a jump of 40,000 in Gatineau. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are also more potential readers in Ottawa , where 393,000 residents claimed working knowledge of French in 2016. &amp;nbsp ; In Gatineau , the total was 302,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not clear how well Le Droit is doing financially , though Fleury was advised that the operation was at least breaking even on operations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If true , that would make Le Droit one of the group&amp;rsquo ; s better performers . According to a Quebec Superior Court ruling in connection with the bankruptcy filing , the six GCM newspapers collectively saw revenues fall 12 per cent per year between 2015 and 2018 , producing net losses of $8 million and $25 million in 2017 and 2018 , respectively . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Losses are expected to continue next year before , optimistically , returning to profits in 2021. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of Nov. 19 , GCM&amp;rsquo ; s liabilities topped $45 million , far outweighing $16 million in assets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the casualties of the transformation from GSM to a co-operative will be the newspapers&amp;rsquo ; retirees , who face 30-per-cent cuts in their pensions as of Jan. 1 . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here , at least , Le Droit retirees should catch a break . They are eligible for assistance of up to $1,500 per month from Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Pension Benefits Guarantee fund . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Quebec does not offer comparable protection . &lt;/p&gt; James BagnallSun , 15 Dec 2019 21:55:46 **45;21782;TOOLONG ... BusinessOttawa 's abysmal year in sports : Why it 's so difficult to build sustainable teams herehttp : **36;21829;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;21867;TOOLONG ... title= " TD Place stadium . " alt= " TD Place stadium . " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;21903;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1521860 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;21933;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Had they understood &lt;em&gt;all&lt;/em&gt; of the risks from the beginning &lt;em&gt; , &lt;/em&gt; they likely would have reconsidered. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the past dozen years , the five founding partners of Ottawa Sports and Entertainment Group , led by real estate mogul Roger Greenberg , have attempted what history suggests is impossible &amp;mdash ; to create a professional sports company capable of sustaining operations in the nation&amp;rsquo ; s capital . And it has cost them dearly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It started out so simply , with the dream of seeing the Canadian Football League return to Lansdowne Park , a few kilometres south of Parliament Hill . Instead , it transformed into a massively complicated $500 million plus project in a 30-year partnership with the city , with nearly all the financial risks borne by the OSEG partners . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the city&amp;rsquo ; s perspective , Lansdowne has been a spectacular win . A football stadium and hockey arena have been restored under the banner of TD Place , and a formerly desolate 40-acre site is now populated by condominiums , office buildings , restaurants , retail shops and an urban park &amp;mdash ; all at no cost to the city thanks largely to the extra realty taxes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The result , financially , looks much different from the vantage of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s partners . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Collectively , OSEG has contributed nearly $100 million more than originally planned to remake Lansdowne . OSEG underestimated the construction costs and failed initially to include sufficient money for food concessions , roof repairs and information technology . The sports and entertainment group is also responsible for absorbing all losses related to operations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On current pace , the partners will lose nearly $70 million of their extra investment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;21969;TOOLONG ... class= " alignnone size-full wp-image-1523056 " src= " https : **34;22004;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 1489 " height= " 1762 " srcset= " https : **31;22040;TOOLONG ... 1489w , https : **39;22073;TOOLONG ... 94w , https : **39;22114;TOOLONG ... 254w , https : **39;22155;TOOLONG ... 768w " sizes= " ( max-width : 1489px ) 100vw , 1489px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The single biggest mistake we made , we did not understand the full extent of the damage in Lansdowne&amp;rsquo ; s infrastructure , &amp;rdquo ; Greenberg said in an interview with this newspaper . &amp;ldquo ; We relied too heavily on disclosures from the city that were not complete . That&amp;rsquo ; s bad on us. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nor is the issue of compromised infrastructure fully accounted for . Seven years into the Lansdowne partnership , OSEG recognizes the entire north side of the football stadium as well as the arena need replacing , and soon . The original assumption had been OSEG would be able to spend a bit each year to keep the stands and arena in good condition . That much was budgeted for , but a full replacement , which would cost tens of millions of dollars , was not . In coming months OSEG and the city will negotiate over who should bear the extra cost . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This , naturally , complicates the job of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s chief executive Mark Goudie , who is trying to achieve breakeven for OSEG by 2022 in order to stop the bleeding . Part of this means attracting at least five million visitors annually to Lansdowne , a million more than this year&amp;rsquo ; s total . Remarkably , fewer than 20 per cent of the visitors need to be football or hockey enthusiasts . While spending by fans is important , modern sports organizations generate the bulk of their money elsewhere . Most of the positive contribution to OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s bottom line comes from rent paid by the retailers and restaurants on the Lansdowne site . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22196;TOOLONG ... title= " Aerial photo of TD Place Stadium and shops at Lansdowne Park . " alt= " Aerial photo of TD Place Stadium and shops at Lansdowne Park . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;22232;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518401 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Embedded in these numbers is the story of how truly difficult it is to build successful franchises in a city with fewer than 1.5 million people , dominated by governments averse to the idea of supporting sports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet , if Goudie actually manages to create a profitable business on behalf of the OSEG partners , Lansdowne could become a model for how to develop Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s largest empty space , LeBreton Flats . It may be , that despite the enormous teething problems , the OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s formula for operating sports franchises is the only one that can succeed here . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; From the beginning , few seemed better suited for the job of reviving professional sports in Ottawa than OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s founders . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three of the partners &amp;mdash ; Roger Greenberg , John Ruddy and Bill Shenkman &amp;mdash ; have serious money , the product of careers spent building homes and commercial properties , many of them in Ottawa . They are familiar with large , complicated projects and the associated risks . The other two partners &amp;mdash ; Jeff Hunt and John Pugh &amp;mdash ; are sports entrepreneurs , former owners respectively of the Ottawa 67&amp;rsquo ; s hockey team and Ottawa Fury soccer franchise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also in their favour , OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s owners have Ottawa in their blood . Greenberg , Ruddy and Shenkman grew up in the city and have a deep , sentimental attachment to Lansdowne Park and the Rough Riders who once played football there . Hunt and Pugh made their way here decades ago from Newfoundland and Britain , respectively . The partners are all well connected in business and philanthropic circles , and at city hall . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These extensive ties are what underpin their willingness to continue despite Lansdowne&amp;rsquo ; s unexpectedly high costs . &amp;nbsp ; For sports fans this matters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s because , with the notable exception of the Ottawa Senators hockey team , owned by pharmaceutical entrepreneur Eugene Melnyk , nearly all of the city&amp;rsquo ; s major sports franchises are either owned by or somehow involved with OSEG , or soon will be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; OSEG owns 100 per cent of the Redblacks and 67&amp;rsquo ; s , the two teams that contribute revenues and expenses to the Lansdowne partnership . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Fury soccer club is owned by Pugh and two other OSEG partners , and operates outside of the partnership . A similar arrangement appears to be in store for the Ottawa Champions baseball club . Several OSEG partners , along with Winnipeg Goldeyes owner Sam Katz , are expected soon to buy the Champions from owner Miles Wolff . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; OSEG also revealed in November that the Ottawa Blackjacks , the seventh franchise of the fledgling Canadian Elite Basketball League , will begin its first season next year at Lansdowne Park . This will see the Blackjacks pay rent to OSEG . It&amp;rsquo ; s likely Ottawa fans will also be able to see professional rugby at Lansdowne starting in 2021. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22262;TOOLONG ... title= " Shanyder Borgelin ( 63 ) of Bethlehem Steel FC gets a handful of jersey as he battles for possession with Onua Obasi of Ottawa Fury FC during a United Soccer League Championship match at TD Place stadium on Saturday , Oct. 12 , 2019. " alt= " Shanyder Borgelin ( 63 ) of Bethlehem Steel FC gets a handful of jersey as he battles for possession with Onua Obasi of Ottawa Fury FC during a United Soccer League Championship match at TD Place stadium on Saturday , Oct. 12 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;22298;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518400 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For those who worry about the evils of corporate concentration , consider that all of the teams touched by OSEG collectively generate less than one-quarter the annual revenues of the Ottawa Senators alone . What all of these teams share is a history of losses &amp;mdash ; a metric that the Senators also dominate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sustaining these teams will mean continued injections of cash from owners , along with cross-subsidies from other businesses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While money-losing sports franchises are hardly unique to Ottawa , owners here face impediments unknown elsewhere in North America . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the biggest obstacles to sports profitability , aside from a small population , is the city&amp;rsquo ; s lopsided economy . Employment in public administration this year has averaged 181,000 &amp;mdash ; nearly 24 per cent of the total workforce for Ottawa-Gatineau . That far exceeds the comparable ratio in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s other large cities . Edmonton , with less than eight per cent of its workforce in government , is a distant second . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The extraordinarily high concentration in Ottawa-Gatineau matters because the federal government , which accounts for nearly 85 per cent of public administration workers here , prohibits employees from accepting free tickets to sports matches to prevent conflicts of interest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In other cities , it&amp;rsquo ; s natural for employers and business owners to invite clients to hockey , baseball or football games . Indeed , the teams&amp;rsquo ; owners count on it , some more than others . An estimated 15 per cent of Redblacks&amp;rsquo ; fans this past year were corporate clients , while for the Ottawa Senators the concentration was 35 per cent , thanks in part to the large number of corporate suites in the Canadian Tire Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even when government employees buy tickets on their own account , the biggest clusters in the capital region happen to live in west Gatineau and east Ottawa &amp;mdash ; many kilometres distant from the NHL arena in Kanata and a **25;22328;TOOLONG commute to Lansdowne . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Add to this a couple of other short-term impediments . First is the multi-year saga involving the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s botched pay system known as Phoenix . Workers uncertain about what amounts will be deposited in their bank accounts , or when this will happen &amp;mdash ; the majority of the government&amp;rsquo ; s workforce , in other words &amp;mdash ; are less likely to spend $100 or more to see the Redblacks or Senators in person . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The extraordinary startup problems experienced since September by Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s new light rail transit system aren&amp;rsquo ; t helping matters . It&amp;rsquo ; s another negative for fans thinking about buying tickets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With any luck , problems with Phoenix and the LRT will be sorted out by next year . But it&amp;rsquo ; s not at all clear that will also be true of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s top sports franchises. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anyway you cut it , this was an abysmal year for Ottawa franchises. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Senators and Redblacks in particular put on a dreadful show , each finishing last in their respective leagues . The NHL squad also ranked 27th out of 31 teams in attendance for the season ended last April , according to ESPN &amp;mdash ; and slumped in the fall to 31st despite rising in the standings . Fewer than 12,000 on average by early December attended games in the 18,500-seat arena . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22355;TOOLONG ... title= " Many empty seats in the Canadian Tire Centre when the Ottawa Senators played the Minnesota Wild . October 14 , 2019. " alt= " Many empty seats in the Canadian Tire Centre when the Ottawa Senators played the Minnesota Wild . October 14 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;22391;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518402 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Redblacks managed to attract more than 22,000 fans to each of their nine home games this year &amp;mdash ; just 2,000 short of capacity , despite losing many of their matches in blowouts . The popularity of the Redblacks owes much to their stellar re-entry into the CFL . Since 2014 , the team has reached the Grey Cup three times , including 2016 when they won the league&amp;rsquo ; s top trophy . Fans can forgive a lousy year after that track record . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But would two lousy years in a row start to undo all of the initial enthusiasm ? OSEG is certainly aware of the risk and determined to reduce it &amp;mdash ; not least because the financial health of the Redblacks is critical to the performance of the Lansdowne partnership . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to snapshots of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s finances obtained by this newspaper , the team for the past couple of seasons has been nudging breakeven on operations on annual revenues of about $21 million . On current projections the team is expected in two years to begin posting strong profits . Over the full 30 years of the Lansdowne partnership , OSEG is counting on the Redblacks for nearly 30 per cent of total earnings on operations . ( The lion&amp;rsquo ; s share of profits will come from net rents contributed by Lansdowne&amp;rsquo ; s retailers. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Financial contributions from the Ottawa 67&amp;rsquo ; s will be substantially less . Although the team was far more competitive than the Redblacks this year &amp;mdash ; they were atop the Ontario Hockey League for the second straight season &amp;mdash ; attendance was shockingly low . It&amp;rsquo ; s been averaging fewer than 4,000 fans per game last year and this fall , according to hockeydb.com , less than half the capacity of the arena . This was the product of a double-whammy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After construction began on the Civic Centre in 2012 , the 67&amp;rsquo ; s were forced to host competitors in the Canadian Tire Centre for a couple of seasons , a longer trek for the 67&amp;rsquo ; s core fan base . Coincidentally , the team&amp;rsquo ; s performance tanked . After finishing first in the east for three straight seasons , the 67&amp;rsquo ; s dropped to fifth in 2013 and 2014 , when they failed to make the playoffs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22421;TOOLONG ... title= " Ottawa 67 's vs Kingston Frontenacs. " alt= " Ottawa 67 's vs Kingston Frontenacs. " data-portal-copyright= " Valerie Wutti " **28;22457;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1367977 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fans stopped going to the games , though whether because of the Kanata location or the team&amp;rsquo ; s record was never quite clear . Even when the 67&amp;rsquo ; s returned to the refurbished Civic Centre in Lansdowne , attendance per game did not climb above 4,000 &amp;mdash ; at least not until the team&amp;rsquo ; s spectacular run in the playoffs this past spring , when they went undefeated until the very last round . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Because the 67&amp;rsquo ; s charge only $30 or so for game tickets , team revenues &amp;mdash ; less than $4 million annually , excluding playoff action &amp;mdash ; are a relatively small piece of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s Lansdowne partnership revenues . Although OSEG is forecasting positive earnings for the team by 2023 or so , these will represent a scant two to three per cent of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s total profits on operations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Professional sports within the city , outside OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s Lansdowne partnership , saw a couple of unsettling developments in 2019 , both directly tied to the vulnerability of franchises in smaller cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s top teams in baseball and soccer &amp;mdash ; the Champions and Fury , respectively &amp;mdash ; suspended operations with little prospect they&amp;rsquo ; ll find a league in which to compete next year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Champions had been playing since 2014 in the independent Can-Am League , which agreed this fall to merge with the Frontier League . Frontier&amp;rsquo ; s teams generally compete in cities in Canada and Northeastern U.S. not associated with Major League Baseball . That makes it tougher to build a base of fans &amp;mdash ; part of the joy of experiencing minor league ball is being able to watch future MLB players before they become famous . Five Can-Am franchises will join Frontier&amp;rsquo ; s nine teams for the first time in 2020 &amp;mdash ; but not the Champions , which will have to wait at least another year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The omission had to do with the uncertainty over the team&amp;rsquo ; s ownership . Miles Wolff had lost a small fortune trying to build a loyal following of Champions fans , and was on the verge of a deal to sell the team to Ottawa businessman Ray Abboud and two of his colleagues . That&amp;rsquo ; s when the agreement to merge the Can-Am and Frontier leagues was announced . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the sale of the Champions was contingent on Abboud and his colleagues signing a lease for use of Raymond Chabot Grant Thornton Park , the city-owned facility across from Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s main VIA train station . And there was a not-so-small complication : the Champions were behind more than $400,000 on their lease payments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the Winnipeg Goldeyes&amp;rsquo ; owner Sam Katz , working together with OSEG , agreed to pick up the lease arrears and inject fresh capital , they became the frontrunners &amp;mdash ; a status confirmed last week by a city council committee , which recommended negotiating a new lease with Katz and OSEG . The purchase price for the franchise will not likely top $1 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Champions are expected to play 2021 in either the Frontier League or Atlantic League , which also features franchises throughout northeast U.S. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just how OSEG and Katz will transform professional baseball into a winner in Ottawa is an open question . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22487;TOOLONG ... title= " A close play at first base in a game between the Ottawa Champions and the Rockland Boulders at RCGT Park on July 15 , 2019. " alt= " A close play at first base in a game between the Ottawa Champions and the Rockland Boulders at RCGT Park on July 15 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;22523;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518403 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It will be a similar period of uncertainty for Ottawa Fury , the soccer franchise that played its first home game at Lansdowne in the summer of 2014. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Fury for the past three seasons had competed in the U.S.-based USL Championship league , the only Canadian team among 36 franchises . The Fury&amp;rsquo ; s value , based on the fee paid by new franchises added in 2019 , was about C$10 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But throughout 2019 , the USL&amp;rsquo ; s governing body &amp;mdash ; the Confederation of North , Central American and Caribbean Association Football , one of six FIFA fiefdoms &amp;mdash ; had been pushing the Fury to join the fledgling Canadian Premier League . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Fury resisted , reckoning its value there would plummet to little more than C$1 million . Not only that , OSEG was concerned that the new Canadian League might not be around in a few years . CONCACAF forced the issue in November by depriving the Fury of its USL Championship credentials . OSEG balked at joining the Canadian league on such terms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The desire to join somebody who has issued you a ransom note was not particularly high , &amp;rdquo ; Goudie said when he announced the suspension of the Fury&amp;rsquo ; s operations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Financially , it may not be much of a penalty . After all , the Fury has suffered average annual losses of $2.5 million since inception . Not playing would save money , in other words . But this is not the way of sports entrepreneurs . The Fury announced Wednesday that it sold its USL franchise rights to Riccardo Silver , the owner of the Miami FC soccer club . Not coincidentally , Miami FC is managed by Paul Dalglish , Fury FC&amp;rsquo ; s former head coach and general manager . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Team suspensions , poor play , low attendance and weak financials &amp;mdash ; all of these hung over Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s sports scene this year . But there was also an extra layer of malaise , triggered by the collapse of what would have been a transformative deal to remake LeBreton Flats . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Construction was to have started this year on a $4-billion project to fill the 55-acre site with condominiums , retail shops , a hotel , museum and other attractions . The centrepiece : an NHL stadium . This was Lansdowne on steroids &amp;mdash ; 10 times the cost , spread over decades , not years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A project this ambitious in a town this small can not succeed without a pooling of resources and deep collaboration . Construction risks are too many , there&amp;rsquo ; s uncertainty over who will buy which condos when , and it&amp;rsquo ; s likely profits won&amp;rsquo ; t emerge until at least a decade after launch . These and other considerations demand a high level of trust between the directing partners . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This was never much in evidence . LeBreton&amp;rsquo ; s two main partners &amp;mdash ; Trinity Development founder John Ruddy and Ottawa Senators owner Eugene Melnyk &amp;mdash ; are embroiled in $1.7 billion worth of lawsuits and counter-claims over how the development should have unfolded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22553;TOOLONG ... title= " Various views of Lebreton Flats area . " alt= " Various views of Lebreton Flats area . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;22589;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1459444 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Part of the dispute appears grounded in disparate personalities . Ruddy is a consummate Ottawa insider . He played cornerback for the St. Patrick&amp;rsquo ; s High School team and , later , for Carleton University&amp;rsquo ; s squad . Ruddy is quiet to the point of self-effacement , nearly always willing to let his business partner Roger Greenberg take the lead in dealing with the public . Colleagues have learned never , ever to interrupt him when he is watching his beloved Redblacks. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk is the mercurial outsider , with his principal home in Barbados . At age 23 he launched a medical publishing company which he sold seven years later in 1989 . Melnyk used the proceeds to create Biovail , which manufactured generic pharmaceuticals and eventually formed the basis of a considerable personal fortune . He bought the Senators out of bankruptcy for the bargain price of US$93 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Typical of many self-made entrepreneurs , Melnyk prefers to do business on his terms . That much was clear during the run up to the Lansdowne project . Those in charge of LeBreton would have been smart to pay attention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Jeff Hunt dropped by to visit Ottawa mayor Larry O&amp;rsquo ; Brien in the fall of 2007 , it had been 10 years since he sold his carpet-cleaning business for a small fortune and nine years since he purchased the 67&amp;rsquo ; s OHL franchise . He was also a die-hard CFL football fan , a season ticket holder until the Rough Riders folded in 1996 , and a fan of the Renegades , which had unexpectedly ceased operations in 2006. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A Thursday night , sitting outside watching CFL football was as much fun as I could have , &amp;rdquo ; he told this newspaper nine years ago , &amp;ldquo ; I was heartbroken when the Rough Riders left . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hunt told O&amp;rsquo ; Brien that he and several partners were eager to bring the CFL back to Ottawa , and sounded out the mayor on the idea of leasing Frank Clair stadium at Lansdowne . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I had no problem with them bringing back football , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Brien recalled in a recent interview . &amp;ldquo ; My problem was this disaster of a site called Lansdowne . I told Jeff that if he came back with a proposal for fixing it , I&amp;rsquo ; d be open to it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A piece of prime property , owned by the city since the mid-1800s , Lansdowne had served as a military staging area , exhibition site and community sports facility . It played host to Stanley Cup matches in 1904 , curling and soccer championships , and several seasons of professional baseball in the 1950s . Most famously , Lansdowne&amp;rsquo ; s Frank Clair stadium was the home for the Ottawa Rough Riders &amp;mdash ; the CFL franchise that so entranced Roger Greenberg and John Ruddy in the late 1960s and 1970s , when the team captured the Grey Cup trophy four times . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But by September 2007 , the stadium and surrounding property were in dangerous disrepair. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After city engineers discovered severe structural damage on the south side stands in September 2007 , the city began examining alternatives for refurbishing the site . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For Hunt , Ruddy , Greenberg and Shenkman , it was an opportunity to revive a favourite CFL franchise and upgrade the home arena for the 67&amp;rsquo ; s . They launched OSEG in 2007 , and the following spring they were awarded a CFL franchise &amp;mdash ; conditional upon a new or refurbished stadium . ( Pugh would join as OSEG partner later. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; OSEG began developing a detailed proposal to remake Lansdowne , which they submitted Oct. 20 , 2008 . The developers in this group knew the sports franchises on their own could not pay for the infrastructure . They would need to include plenty of housing and retail shops to create an economic engine that would sustain the entire project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22619;TOOLONG ... title= " Dominique Rhymes #15 of the Ottawa Redblacks is unable to make the catch against the Toronto Argonauts during a CFL game held at TD Place on Sept. 7 , 2019. " alt= " Dominique Rhymes #15 of the Ottawa Redblacks is unable to make the catch against the Toronto Argonauts during a CFL game held at TD Place on Sept. 7 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jana Chytilova " **28;22655;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1486955 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meanwhile , Eugene Melnyk was preparing to make a pitch for a Major League Soccer franchise . At the time , Toronto was the only Canadian city with a team in the top-ranked league but MLS commissioner Don Garber confirmed in July 2008 that Ottawa was one of a handful of contenders for two new franchises to be awarded in 2011. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In September , Melnyk and Ottawa Senators president Cyril Leeder unveiled plans for a new $100 million plus MLS stadium to be built in Kanata on city land near the site of the Senators&amp;rsquo ; home arena , the Canadian Tire Centre . Eventually , it would be surrounded by condos , apartments , offices and retail shops . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was a caveat , of course . Melnyk was seeking financing from all three levels of government &amp;mdash ; $37 million each from the feds and the province and $17 million from the city . Melnyk would put down $10 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The city made clear it was prepared to provide some support for either the Kanata stadium or Lansdowne facility , but not both , and possibly not either . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Behind the scenes , O&amp;rsquo ; Brien proposed a compromise &amp;mdash ; a dual-sport stadium for Kanata . The city would contribute land and possibly other assistance for the 20,000-seat soccer facility . The new CFL franchise would rent it . &amp;ldquo ; The idea was to redo Lansdowne , but move football to Kanata , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Brien said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A tentative deal emerged in late March or early April 2009 after a rapid fire series of phone calls between O&amp;rsquo ; Brien , Leeder and various members of OSEG . Two principals involved in the talks said OSEG agreed to rent the MLS stadium for at least nine regular season CFL games . The city would refurbish the Lansdowne site on a smaller scale , without a stadium . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although the MLS in March had already announced Portland and Vancouver would be awarded the two new franchises for 2011 , it was understood the league would continue to expand . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In April 2009 , O&amp;rsquo ; Brien called Leeder with the news he would support the two-sport stadium concept but was somewhat puzzled when a delighted Leeder wrapped up the call by telling him , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ll just run this by the boss . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk , it turned out , wasn&amp;rsquo ; t happy with the news at all . &amp;ldquo ; Eugene doesn&amp;rsquo ; t want to do it , &amp;rdquo ; a very apologetic Leeder told O&amp;rsquo ; Brien shortly after . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stunned at the turn of events , O&amp;rsquo ; Brien called Melnyk at his home in Barbados and asked why the Senators&amp;rsquo ; owner did not want to collaborate on a two-sport stadium . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He said &amp;lsquo ; the CFL is a relic&amp;rsquo ; and that he wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t want to share his MLS stadium with the CFL , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Brien recalled . &amp;ldquo ; I reminded him that this wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be &lt;em&gt;his&lt;/em&gt; stadium . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the details were yet to be worked out , Ottawa would co-finance with other governments the building of what would be a city facility . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22685;TOOLONG ... title= " Views of TD Place Stadium Sunday December 1 , 2019. " alt= " Views of TD Place Stadium Sunday December 1 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;22721;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1518404 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s partners went back to &amp;ldquo ; Plan A&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; a full remake of Lansdowne Park . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After Melnyk dismissed O&amp;rsquo ; Brien&amp;rsquo ; s offer of supporting the MLS soccer stadium , the mayor lobbied every councillor he could find in favour of OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s proposal , which prevailed by a vote of 14 to nine on April 22 , 2009 , in a meeting of the full city council . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk dropped his pursuit of MLS soccer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk declined comment on his decade-old conversation with O&amp;rsquo ; Brien , but noted in an email that his proposed stadium was not soccer exclusive , and that he &amp;ldquo ; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;remains disappointed that our proposal was n't selected by council . " The Senators owner added : " We remain confident our proposal was the correct business decision , especially in light of how soccer attendance , team values and expansion fees have skyrocketed as the MLS has grown over the past 10 years . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s something few could have predicated in 2009 , the year of the global financial crisis . The MLS entry fee that year was about US$35 million to US$40 million . A decade later , the next expansion club is expected to shell out more than US$300 million according to Forbes Magazine &lt;strong&gt;. &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Was Ruddy paying close attention ? Five years later , in the fall of 2014 , the Trinity Development founder met with Melnyk to discuss , for the first time , the possibility of joining forces in a bid to develop LeBreton Flats , land owned by the federal National Capital Commission . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between January 2015 &amp;mdash ; when Melnyk and Ruddy submitted their bid under the name RendezVous LeBreton &amp;mdash ; and April 2015 , when they were selected as one of four finalists , Melnyk was readying for a liver transplant . He received it May 19 , 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For much of this period , Cyril Leeder took the lead in negotiating the terms of the partnership , though Melnyk appears to have been kept appraised of all key developments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , as Melnyk recovered , his instincts to assume control re-emerged . He also grew more concerned about the financial risks &amp;mdash ; which he believed were exacerbated by a separate Ruddy project to build three large towers of apartments adjacent to the LeBreton Flats site . Leeder had believed the project would help create a lively atmosphere in the LeBreton area , injecting a critical mass of people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk didn&amp;rsquo ; t see it that way . He became convinced the development at 900 Albert St. would fatally injure the economics of LeBreton Flats , which depended on the sale of residential and retail properties . The units at 900 Albert St. were to be marketed to the public first . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk&amp;rsquo ; s reduced financial heft may have been a factor in his caution . When he purchased the Senators in 2003 , the value of his stake in Biovail was a tidy $1.7 billion . One year later , his Biovail stock was worth $600 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;22751;TOOLONG ... title= " Eugene Melnyk. " alt= " Eugene Melnyk. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;22787;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1480036 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The slide continued in May 2009 , when Melnyk lost control of Biovail&amp;rsquo ; s board of directors . Melnyk sold off his entire stake for several hundred million dollars , based on the average Biovail share price during this period . Had he held his shares another few years , they would have been worth close to $6 billion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The loss of wealth hurt , especially considering the weak economics of the Ottawa Senators . From 2003 to 2013 , despite a highly competitive team , the Senators lost nearly $10 million per year . Part of this reflected the difficulty of raising ticket prices in the Ottawa market . At an average price of less than US$60 per ticket , the Senators can charge less than half the amount per ticket as the Toronto Maple Leafs . Not only is Ottawa-Gatineau one-fifth the size of greater Toronto in population , the Senators must lure fans deep into the west end while Toronto fans can take the subway downtown . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And there&amp;rsquo ; s another problem unique to the Senators &amp;mdash ; Ottawa is knee deep in hockey fans , but too many of them maintain tribal loyalties with teams from Montreal and Toronto . These allegiances were formed long before the Senators re-entered the league in the early 1990s and have proved enduring . Canadiens or Maple Leafs fans will help fill the Canadian Tire Centre no matter the distance or weather , but are less enthusiastic when other teams are featured . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Melnyk nevertheless has benefitted hugely from the increase in the overall value of the team , from US$93 million when he bought it to US$435 million last year , according to a recent Forbes Magazine estimate . Over the same period , the Senators have seen team debt increase from US$40 million to roughly US$200 million , as Melnyk borrowed to cover the team&amp;rsquo ; s annual losses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That much at least , is something OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s partners can only envy . They did not have the luxury of solid capital gains when it came to negotiating with the city over the terms of the Lansdowne project . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When OSEG and the city formally concluded their partnership in 2012 , the objective was pretty straightforward . Each would pay for specific parts of the project . The city , for instance would finance the stadium , arena , part of the parking lot and the urban park . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; OSEG would pay for the rest of the parking lot and the retail shops . It would also be responsible for operating the 30-year Lansdowne partnership with the city , which means absorbing the annual losses in the early years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The idea was to pool streams of cash from ticket sales , arena and stadium rentals , parking and concessions . These were to be allocated first to OSEG , then the city , according to a formula . OSEG for instance is permitted to earn 8 per cent simple return on the amount it invests ( equivalent to less than 5 per cent compounded ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But note the difference in how these partners financed their particular piece . The city in 2013 issued a sinking fund debenture &amp;mdash ; a loan whose principal drops over time &amp;mdash ; for $154 million . This was secured against realty taxes paid by Lansdowne&amp;rsquo ; s retail shops among other new tenants , as well as air rights paid by Minto in order to erect condo towers on site . In financing , this is about as low risk as you can get . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the other hand , OSEG estimates it will have contributed equity of $152 million over the life the project , nearly $100 million more than expected . &amp;nbsp ; All of this money comes straight out of the pocket of the five partners , but you can be sure Greenberg and Ruddy have paid the bulk . Since they are entitled to a return on their extra investment , they won&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily incur an actual loss . This will depend in part on Goudie&amp;rsquo ; s success in generating new streams of revenue from the Lansdowne site , and how much new investment will be required to make sure the stadium and arena be up to code by 2044 , when the partnership terminates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s more than possible OSEG will wind up with a total return of zero , underscoring the punishing economics of sports in the nation&amp;rsquo ; s capital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Few people feel sorry for these guys . They&amp;rsquo ; ve done well by their businesses in the city . OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s partners went into the Lansdowne project as a way of giving something back to the community . In the end they did this by bringing a derelict urban site back to life . They also got their CFL team back , along with a bunch of other professional sports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Had they known then what they know now , it&amp;rsquo ; s possible they might have balked at what it all cost . But they&amp;rsquo ; re in it now , and have every incentive to come up with a formula for cross subsidizing sports teams in a manner that&amp;rsquo ; s fair to owners as well as taxpayers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Much rides on OSEG&amp;rsquo ; s shoulders , perhaps even the future of professional sports in Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;22817;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22859;TOOLONG ... unlikely to get back into soccer , Fury FC franchise rights sold to **26;22894;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;22922;TOOLONG ... reveals LeBreton draft master plan , as the redesigning **27;22957;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; James BagnallThu , 12 Dec 2019 22:21:08 **45;22986;TOOLONG ... BusinessLocal NewsEnergy does n't divide Canadians as sharply as we think , poll sayshttp : **36;23284;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;23322;TOOLONG ... title= " Monica Gattinger is a political scientist talking about energy policy and climate change . " alt= " Monica Gattinger is a political scientist talking about energy policy and climate change . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;23358;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1520641 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadians&amp;rsquo ; views on energy and climate changes aren&amp;rsquo ; t as polarized as we may think , says a new poll , leaving hope that while our opinions are &amp;ldquo ; fragmented , &amp;rdquo ; we have room to work things out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Take the hot-button topic of building pipelines , says political scientist Monica Gattinger . Her research group&amp;rsquo ; s new survey found that while opinions are split , there aren&amp;rsquo ; t huge numbers of &amp;ldquo ; views that are hardened at either end of the spectrum . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead she says there are &amp;ldquo ; differences of opinion that might be a little more evenly distributed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That actually gives me some hope in terms of people&amp;rsquo ; s views&amp;rsquo ; being more malleable , more open to compromise . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These findings come from a poll commissioned by Positive Energy , Gattinger&amp;rsquo ; s research group at the University of Ottawa that studies energy policy . It is being &lt;a href= " https : **33;23388;TOOLONG ... online&lt;/a&gt; on Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; On both carbon tax and pipelines , when we look at the national ( results ) what we see is fragmented opinion&amp;rdquo ; but not polarized extremes , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even across regional and age groups , &amp;ldquo ; we&amp;rsquo ; re not seeing polarized views in either of those cases , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall the survey of 2,679 adults found that 51 per cent agree that Canada needs a carbon tax and 36 per cent disagree . On pipelines , 50 per cent think their benefits outweigh risks and 35 per cent disagree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she said results were not tilted toward extremes of strongly agreeing or disagreeing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gattinger&amp;rsquo ; s group plans to run this survey repeatedly over time to track changes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What I am most enthusiastic about is the ability of this survey to unpack these issues around polarization , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are very excited to get this material out into the public domain , to those in government and those tracking climate issue , but also to individual Canadians as well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The survey asks a lot about people&amp;rsquo ; s values and world views , which she says will also help to understand the public . &amp;ldquo ; That could help to further understand what is going on in the country when it comes to debates on energy and climate . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The survey also found that we hold some contradictory ideas about energy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Respondents told the pollsters that building pipelines is not compatible with reaching our climate goals . But they also see them as beneficial and want to build more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then there&amp;rsquo ; s clean energy . Respondents want to reduce greenhouse gases from cheap , traditional sources . But they also say that affordability is their top energy priority . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Gattinger said the problem is that we are both citizens and consumers . &amp;ldquo ; When they think as citizens , yes of course they are very concerned about the climate and the need to reduce emissions . But sometimes when they think as consumers , their interests might run to a different direction . &amp;rdquo ; As consumers , Canadians confront political leaders &amp;ldquo ; forcefully&amp;rdquo ; over high energy prices , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not surprising to find inconsistency in people&amp;rsquo ; s beliefs , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s where the political **26;23423;TOOLONG ; needs to do a better job of articulating what some of the trade-offs are , and making policy choices that are informed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; How to chart Canada&amp;rsquo ; s energy future in an age of climate change is particularly fraught , because sometimes energy and climate imperatives point in different directions . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report is by Gattinger and two other political scientists , Erick Lachapelle of the Universit&amp;eacute ; de Montr&amp;eacute ; al and Stephen Bird at Clarkson University in New York . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is no margin of error for the survey as it was done with a panel of respondents already registered with a survey company and is not technically a random survey . &lt;/p&gt; " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;24188;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1343659 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; biggest little coffee company , &amp;rdquo ; Bridgehead has been sold to the owners of the Second Cup coffee chain in a deal worth $9.5 million . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bridgehead has 19 coffeehouses in the capital , including its landmark Roastery on Anderson Street near Preston . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bridgehead was founded in 1981 , when two United Church ministers and two social activists concerned for the prospects of small-scale coffee farmers in Nicaragua formed Bridgehead Trading , the companies said in a release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bridgehead became the first company in Canada to offer consumers fairly traded coffee when volunteers sold Bridgehead coffee from church basements . The brand was acquired by OxFam Canada , which diversified the operation to include a broader cross-section of fairly-traded products . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Current owner Tracey Clark bought the brand in 2000 , and returned the company to &amp;ldquo ; its roots as a coffee and tea company , with a focus on quality and sustainability . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have long had a goal of being the biggest little coffee company , &amp;rdquo ; Clark said in the news release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Joining Aegis gives us access to the resources to expand our footprint without needing to change who we are or what we do best : advocating for small scale farmers , offering coffee that is well-sourced , well-roasted and well-served , creating a sense of belonging for our customers and employees , and fostering community connections . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Clark will continue as Bridgehead&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; chief culture officer , &amp;rdquo ; while Ian Clark , director of coffee , and Cliff Hansen , the company&amp;rsquo ; s roastmaster , will continue in their roles . Bridgehead&amp;rsquo ; s head office will remain in Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bridgehead is Second Cup&amp;rsquo ; s first acquisition under its new corporate entity Aegis Brands , which comes into full effect in 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Bridgehead is the quintessential &amp;lsquo ; amazing brand&amp;rsquo ; &amp;mdash ; a company with a deep sense of purpose , strong roots in its community and an incredible potential for growth , &amp;rdquo ; Aegis Brands CEO Steve Pelton said in the release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aegis will pay $6 million in cash and $3.5 million worth of Second Cup shares . An additional payment of $1.5 million will be paid if Bridgehead meets unspecified profit targets over the next two years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The deal is expected to close this month . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;24218;TOOLONG ...
